102.13985pt\raise-40.55113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

i.e.,
\Re(B)^{0}
is the stack theoretic intersection of the closed substacks
\Re(B)^{+}
and
\Re(B)^{-}
. We also define for
?\in\{+,-,0\}
open substacks of
\Re(B)
by

\Re(B)^{\neq?}:=\Re(B)\setminus\Re(B)^{?}.

Then

\Re(B)^{\neq 0}=\Re(B)^{\neq+}\cup\Re(B)^{\neq-}.

Since
B^{+}=B/(t)
, we have a cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\Re(B)^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Re(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Re(B)^{\neq+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\Fil}}
\textstyle{\Spec A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the right vertical arrow is an isomorphism since we can also describe
\Re(B)^{\neq+}
as the locus where
t
is invertible in
\Re(B)
and (
2.2.2
) yields an isomorphism

(2.2.9)

\Spec A=\Spec B_{-\infty}\overset{\sim}{\to}\Re(B)^{\neq+}.

Composing this isomorphism with the inclusion
\Re(B)^{\neq+}\to\Re(B)
is then the map
\tau
defined in (
2.2.4
).

Remark 2.14
.

Let
(A,(\Fil_{A}^{j})_{j})
be a filtered ring and let
\psi\colon A\to C
be a ring homomorphism. Set
\Fil^{j}_{C}:=C\otimes_{A}\Fil^{j}_{A}
for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
with induced maps
t\colon\Fil_{C}^{j+1}\to\Fil_{C}^{j}
. Then
(C,(\Fil_{C}^{j})_{j})
is again a filtered ring, denoted by
\psi^{*}(A,(\Fil_{A}^{j})_{j})
.

Let
\Re(A)
and
\Re(C)
be the corresponding Rees stacks. Then one has a 2-cartesian diagram of algebraic stacks

\textstyle{\Re(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Re(\psi)}
\textstyle{\Re(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{\Fil}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\Fil}}
\textstyle{A^{\Fil}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Spec\psi}
\textstyle{\Spec A.}

Similarly, one has

(2.2.10)

\displaystyle\Re(C)^{?}

\displaystyle=C\otimes_{A}\Re(A)^{?},

\displaystyle\Re(C)^{\neq?}

\displaystyle=C\otimes_{A}\Re(A)^{\neq?}

for
?\in\{+,-,0\}
.

Definition and Remark 2.15
.

A
map of filtered rings

(A(\Fil^{j}_{A}))\to(C,(\Fil^{j}_{C}))
is a pair consisting of a map
\psi\colon A\to C
of rings and a map of filtered
C
-algebras
\psi_{\bullet}\colon\psi^{*}(A,(\Fil^{j}_{A}))\to(C,(\Fil^{j}_{C}))
.

Equivalently, a map of filtered rings is given by a ring homomorphism
\psi\colon A\to C
and by
A
-linear maps
\psi_{j}\colon\Fil^{j}_{A}\to\Fil^{j}_{C}
for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\psi_{0}=\psi
and such that for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
the diagram

\textstyle{\Fil^{j+1}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{j+1}}
\textstyle{\Fil^{j}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{j}}
\textstyle{\Fil^{j+1}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\Fil^{j}_{C}}

commutes.

2.3.
Filtered modules over filtered rings

Definition 2.16
.

Let
(A,(\Fil^{j}))
be a filtered ring and let
\Re(A)
be the attached Rees stack. Then a
filtered module over
(A,(\Fil^{j}))
is a quasi-cohrent module on
\Re(A)
.

Then a filtered
(A,(\Fil^{j}))
-module can be also described as a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A
-module
M=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{i}
endowed with the following additional structure.

(a)

For all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
an
A
-linear map
t\colon M_{i}\to M_{i-1}
and

(b)

for all
j>0
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
an
A
-linear map
\Fil^{j}\otimes_{A}M_{i}\to M_{i+j}
such that the diagram

\textstyle{\Fil^{j}\otimes_{A}M_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{i+j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\Fil^{j-1}\otimes_{A}M_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{i+j-1}}

commutes, where the left vertical map is induced by
\Fil^{j}\to\Fil^{j-1}
.

We usually write
(M_{i+1}\to M_{i})_{i}
for such a datum.

We use the notation of Section
1.3
. In particular, for
e\in\mathbb{Z}
we have the line bundle
{\mathscr{O}}_{\Re(A)}(e)
on
\Re(A)
. It corresponds to
M=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{j}
with
M_{j}=\Fil^{i+e}
.

For a general quasi-coherent module
{\mathscr{E}}
over
\Re(A)
we set

{\mathscr{E}}(e):={\mathscr{E}}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{\Re(A)}(e).

If
{\mathscr{E}}
is a quasi-coherent module on
\Re(A)
with corresponding graded module
M=\bigoplus M_{j}
, then
\Gamma(\Re(A),{\mathscr{E}})=M_{0}
and more generally
\Gamma(\Re(A),{\mathscr{E}}(j))=M_{j}
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
by (
1.3.1
).

By Proposition
1.6
, a quasi-coherent module
{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Re(A)
is a vector bundle on
\Re(A)
if and only if
{\mathscr{E}}
is a direct summand of a module of the form of a finite direct sum
\bigoplus_{i}{\mathscr{O}}(e_{i})
for finitely many
e_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

3.
The display group

3.1.
Extension and liftings of
G
-bundles

We will use the following two special cases of results in
[
Wed24
]
. We continue to denote by
A
a ring.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
B=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}B_{i}
be a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded ring and set
J:=\sum_{i>0}B_{i}B_{-i}\subseteq B_{0}
. Then we call
B

henselian
if
(B_{0},J)
is a henselian pair.

For instance, every
\mathbb{Z}
-graded ring
B
with
B_{i}=0
for
i<0
is henselian.

Theorem 3.2
.

(
[
Wed24
, 2.6]
)
Let
B
be a henselian
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A
-algebra, set
X:=\Spec B
,
{\mathcal{X}}:=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash X]
, and
R:=B_{0}/\sum_{i>0}B_{i}B_{-i}
. Let
X^{+}
,
X^{-}
, and
X^{0}=\Spec R
be the attractor, the repeller, and the fixed point locus, of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-scheme
X
respectively. Set
{\mathcal{X}}^{?}:=[\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash X^{?}]
for
?\in\{+,-,0\}
. In particular
{\mathcal{X}}^{0}=B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
.

Let
G
be an affine smooth group scheme over
A
. Then pullback by the inclusions induces full and essentially surjective morphisms of groupoids

\textstyle{\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{X}}^{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R})}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{X}}^{-})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

In particular we have isomorphism of pointed sets

H^{1}({\mathcal{X}},G)\cong H^{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{\pm},G)\cong H^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R},G).

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
(A,(\Fil^{j}))
be a filtered ring (Definition
2.7
) such that
(A,t(\Fil^{1}))
is a henselian pair. Set
R:=A/t(\Fil^{1})
. Then for every smooth affine group scheme
G
over
A
, one has bijections

(3.1.1)

H^{1}(\Re(A),G)\cong H^{1}(\Re(A)^{+},G)\cong H^{1}(\Re(A)^{-},G)\cong H^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}).

Theorem 3.4
.

(
[
Wed24
, 1.7, 1.13]
)
Let
B
be a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A
-algebra, set
X:=\Spec B
and
{\mathcal{X}}:=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash X]
. Let
j\colon U\to X
be the inclusion of an open quasi-compact
\mathbb{G}_{m,A}
-invariant subscheme such that
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\overset{\sim}{\to}j_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{U}
.

(1)

For every affine flat group scheme
G
over
A
, the pullback

j^{*}\colon\Bun_{G}([\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash X])\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}([\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash U])

is fully faithful.

(2)

If
X
is regular of dimension
2
and
U
contains every point of codimension
\leq 1
of
X
and if
G
is a reductive group scheme over
A
, then
j^{*}
is an equivalence of groupoids.

In Theorem
3.4

(2)
it suffices that
G
is geometrically reductive in the sense of
[
Alp14
, Section 9]
.

3.2.
The stack of types of
G
-bundles for a reductive group scheme
G

Let
S
be an algebraic space and let
G
be a smooth affine group algebraic space over
S
.

The groupoid
\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
can be described as the groupoid of pairs
({\mathscr{E}},\lambda)
, where
{\mathscr{E}}
is a
G
-bundle on
S
and where
\lambda\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,S}\to\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}})
is a cocharacter by
[
Wed24
, A.36]
. A morphism
({\mathscr{E}},\lambda)\to({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})
in
\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
is an isomorphism
{\mathscr{E}}\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
of
G
-bundles intertwining
\lambda
and
\lambda^{\prime}
.

Definition 3.5
.

The stack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
on
\mathcal{A}\textup{ff/$S$}
that sends an affine
S
-scheme
T
to the groupoid
\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,T})
is called the
stack of types of
G
-bundles
.

Recall that we have the following alternative description of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
.

3.6
.

Let

X_{*}(G):=\Hom_{\textup{$S$-Grp}}(\mathbb{G}_{m,S},G),\qquad\underline{X}_{*}(G):=\underline{\Hom}_{\textup{$S$-Grp}}(\mathbb{G}_{m,S},G)

be the set (resp. sheaf) of cocharacters of
G
. By
[
SGA64
, XV, 8.11]
,
\underline{X}_{*}(G)
is representable by an algebraic space which is surjective, smooth, and separated over
S
. Since
G
is affine over
S
, the algebraic space
\underline{X}_{*}(G)
is ind-quasi-affine over
S
by
[
Cot23
, 5.8]
. Moreover,
G
acts on
\underline{X}_{*}(G)
by conjugation from the left and sending a cocharacter
\mu
of
G
to the pair
(P_{0},\mu)
, where
P_{0}
is the trivial
G
-bundle, yields an isomorphism

[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)]\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})

by
[
Wed24
, Section A.7]
. In particular, the stack of types
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
is a smooth algebraic stack with affine diagonal.

3.7
Coarse moduli space of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{G}
be the coarse fppf-sheaf of
[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)]
, i.e., the fppf-sheafification of the presheaf on
\mathcal{A}\textup{ff/$S$}
that attaches to an affine
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
the set of isomorphism classes of
[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)](S^{\prime})
. Since
G\to S
and
\underline{X}_{*}(G)\to S
are both fppf-covering, so is the inertia stack

{\mathcal{I}}_{[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)]}=[G\backslash(G\times_{S}\underline{X}_{*}(G))]\longrightarrow[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)].

Therefore
{\mathcal{C}}_{G}
is a smooth algebraic space over
S
(and in particular an fpqc-sheaf by Gabber’s theorem) and
[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)]\to{\mathcal{C}}_{G}
is smooth and a gerbe (lacking a better reference: apply
[
AOV08
, A.1]
to the rigidification obtained by “removing” the inertia subgroup stack itself). For every geometric point
\bar{s}\to S
we have

H^{1}(G_{\bar{s}},B\mathbb{G}_{m,\bar{s}})={\mathcal{C}}_{G}(\bar{s}).

Definition and Remark 3.8
.

Let
[\mu]\in[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)](S^{\prime})
be a conjugacy class of cocharacters defined over some
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
.

(1)

The corresponding
G
-bundle over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
will be denoted by
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
. If
[\mu]
can be represented by some cocharacter
\mu
of
G
, we also write
{\mathscr{E}}_{\mu}
instead of
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
.

(2)

Also define the relatively affine auutomorphism group stack of
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
as

(3.2.1)

[\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}}:=\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}).

Again, we write
\mu\backslash G_{S^{\prime}}
if
[\mu]
is represented by a cocharacter
\mu
.

Then
[\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}}
is a group stack over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
sitting in a 2-cartesian diagram

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.18022pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.18022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 35.18022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-23.99109pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.18022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 35.58061pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

If
[\mu]
can be represented by a cocharacter
\mu
of
G_{S^{\prime}}
, then
\mu\backslash G_{S^{\prime}}
is the quotient stack of
G_{S^{\prime}}
by the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action via conjugation with
\mu
.

We may view
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
also as the trivial
[\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}}
-bundle via twisting
[
Wed24
, (A.6.1)]
.

(3)

For every
S^{\prime}
-scheme
T
we define
\Bun_{G}^{[\mu]}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,T})
to be the full subgroupoid of
\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,T})
of
G
-bundles that are étale locally on
T
isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}{}_{|}{}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,T}}
. These
G
-bundles are said to be
of type
[\mu]
.

More generally, let
{\mathcal{X}}
be any stack over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
. Then a
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{F}}
on
{\mathcal{X}}
is said to be
of type
[\mu]
(or
of type
\mu
if a representing cocharacter
\mu
of
[\mu]
is chosen) if there exists an étale covering
{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{X}}
such that
{\mathscr{F}}{}_{|}{}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}\cong{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}{}_{|}{}_{{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}
.

(4)

Let
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
be the substack of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
sending an
S^{\prime}
-scheme
T
to
\Bun^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,T})
.

Remark 3.9
.

Occasionally, we will apply this construction of
[\mu]\backslash G
to the more general case that
[\mu]
is an
S^{\prime}
-valued point of the quotient stack
[G\backslash\underline{\Hom}(\mathbb{G}_{m,S},\underline{\Aut}(G))]
, where
G
acts via conjugaction, i.e.,
[\mu]
is a
G
-conjugacy class of cocharacters of
\underline{\Aut}(G)
. In this case we define
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
as the trivial
[\mu]\backslash G
-bundle over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
.

Proposition 3.10
.

With keep the notation as in Definition
3.8
. Then the following assertions hold

(1)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
is a neutral gerbe. More precisely, there exists a smooth affine group algebraic space
L
over
S^{\prime}
, an étale covering
S^{\prime\prime}
of
S^{\prime}
, a cocharacter
\mu
of
G_{S^{\prime\prime}}
representing
[\mu]
, and an isomorphism
L_{S^{\prime\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\Cent_{G_{S^{\prime\prime}}}(\mu)
of group algebraic spaces over
S^{\prime\prime}
such that

(3.2.3)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})=BL.

(2)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
is a smooth quasi-compact algebraic fpqc-stack with affine diagonal over
S^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Set
{\mathcal{X}}:=\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
and let
L:=\underline{\Aut}_{{\mathcal{X}}}([\mu])
be the group algebraic space of automorphisms of the
S^{\prime}
-valued point
[\mu]
of
{\mathcal{X}}
. Then the natural monomorphism
BL\to{\mathcal{X}}
induces by
[
Wed24
, A.5]
an isomorphism
BL\overset{\sim}{\to}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
. Now choose an étale covering
S^{\prime\prime}\to S^{\prime}
such that
[\mu]
can be represented over
S^{\prime\prime}
by a cocharacter
\mu
of
G_{S^{\prime\prime}}
. Since
\underline{\Aut}(G_{S^{\prime\prime}},\mu)=\Cent_{G_{S^{\prime\prime}}}(\mu)
we find
L_{S^{\prime\prime}}\cong\Cent_{G_{S^{\prime\prime}}}(\mu)
. Since
\Cent_{G_{S^{\prime\prime}}}(\mu)
is a smooth affine algebraic group space over
S^{\prime\prime}
(Remark
1.5
), so is
L
over
S^{\prime}
by descent. In particular
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{\mu}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})=BL
is a smooth quasi-compact algebraic fpqc-stack with affine diagonal.
∎

There is the following alternative definition of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})
.

Definition and Remark 3.11
.

Let
S^{\prime}
be an
S
-scheme and let
[\mu]\in[G\backslash\underline{X}_{*}(G)](S^{\prime})
. Define a stack
B([\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}})
over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
as follows. If
T
is a scheme and
f\colon T\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
let
B([\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}})(T)
be the groupoid of
f^{*}([\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}})
-bundles over
T
. By (
3.2.2
) there is a 2-cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B([\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}.}

Since
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})\to S^{\prime}
is a smooth algebraic gerbe with affine diagonal (Proposition
3.10
), so is
B([\mu]\backslash G_{S^{\prime}})\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}
.

From now on suppose that
G
is reductive over
S
, i.e.,
G\to S
is affine and smooth with connected reductive geometric fibers.

Remark and Definition 3.12
.

Locally for the étale topology on
S
one can choose a pinning of
G

[
DG64
, XXV, 1.1]
which in turn yields a based root datum
(X^{*},\Phi,X_{*},\Phi{}^{\vee},\Delta)
. By descent, this allows to attach to
G
globally a tuple of étale locally constant schemes over
S
which we denote by
(\underline{X}^{*},\underline{\Phi},\underline{X}_{*},\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee},\underline{\Delta})
and call it
the
based root datum of
G
. By definition of a based root datum, this quintuple of locally constant schemes is endowed with additional structures:
\underline{X}^{*}
and
\underline{X}_{*}
are locally constant sheaves that are vector bundles over the constant sheaf of rings
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
and there is given a perfect pairing of
\underline{X}^{*}
and
\underline{X}_{*}
with values in
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
. The schemes
\underline{\Phi}
,
\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee}
, and
\underline{\Delta}
are each locally constant finite
S
-schemes, i.e. finite étale
S
-schemes. There are given open and closed immersions
\underline{\Delta}\to\underline{\Phi}\to\underline{X}^{*}
and
\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee}\to\underline{X}_{*}
and an isomorphism
\underline{\Phi}\overset{\sim}{\to}\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee}
of locally constant sheaves denoted by
\alpha\mapsto\alpha{}^{\vee}
. All these data have to satisfy the conditions of a based root datum
[
DG64
, XXI, 1.1.1, 3.1.6]
.
If
S^{\prime}\to S
is an étale covering such that
G
is split over
S^{\prime}
, then the choice of a split Borel pair
T\subseteq B\subseteq G_{S^{\prime}}
yields a canonical isomorphism of
(\underline{X}^{*}(S^{\prime}),\underline{\Phi}(S^{\prime}),\underline{X}_{*}(S^{\prime}),\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee}(S^{\prime}),\underline{\Delta}(S^{\prime}))
with the based root datum of
G
defined by
(T,B)
.

To the based root datum of
G
one attaches its Weyl group
\underline{W}_{G}
. It is a finite étale group scheme. It is equipped with an open and closed immersion of an
S
-scheme
\underline{I}\to\underline{W}_{G}
, the scheme of simple reflections in
\underline{W}_{G}
. The finite étale group scheme
\underline{W}_{G}
acts on
\underline{X}^{*}
and
\underline{X}_{*}
. The choice of a split Borel pair
(T,B)
over an étale covering
S^{\prime}\to S
yields an isomorphism
(\underline{W}_{G},\underline{I})\times_{S}S^{\prime}\cong(\Norm_{G_{S}^{\prime}}(T)/T,I_{(B,T)})
over
S^{\prime}
by
[
DG64
, XXII, 3.4]
, where
I_{(B,T)}
is the set of simple reflections defined by
B
in
\Norm_{G_{S}^{\prime}}(T)/T
.

Attaching to
s\in S
the based root datum
(\underline{X}^{*}(\bar{s}),\underline{\Phi}(\bar{s}),\underline{X}_{*}(\bar{s}),\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee}(\bar{s}),\underline{\Delta}(\bar{s}))
, where
\bar{s}
is some geometric point lying over
s
, defines a locally constant map from the underlying topological space of
S
to the set of isomorphism classes of based root data.

To simplify our notation, we will in the sequel always assume that this map is constant.
We call the isomorphism class of
(\underline{X}^{*}(\bar{s}),\underline{\Phi}(\bar{s}),\underline{X}_{*}(\bar{s}),\underline{\Phi}{}^{\vee}(\bar{s}),\underline{\Delta}(\bar{s}))

the geometric based root datum
of
G
and usually denote it simply by
(X^{*},\Phi,X_{*},\Phi{}^{\vee},\Delta)
. Its Weyl group is given by
W_{G}:=\underline{W}_{G}(\bar{s})
, and it is called
the geometric Weyl group
.

3.13
.

Every cocharacter
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G
of the reductive group scheme
G
, possibly defined over some étale covering of
S
, factorizes locally for the étale topology through some maximal torus
T
. Hence we obtain for the sheaf of conjugacy classes of cocharacters an identification

{\mathcal{C}}_{G}=\underline{W}_{G}\backslash\underline{X}_{*},

where we denote by
\underline{W}_{G}\backslash\underline{X}_{*}
the étale sheaf quotient (or, equivalently, the fppf-sheaf quotient) of the action of the finite group scheme
\underline{W}_{G}
on the scheme
\underline{X}_{*}
. Since
\underline{W}_{G}
and
\underline{X}_{*}
are both locally for the étale topology constant, so is
{\mathcal{C}}_{G}
. In particular, it is represented by an étale and ind-quasi-affine algebraic space over
S
.

Attaching to
s\in S
the set
{\mathcal{C}}_{G}(\bar{s})=W_{G}(T)(\bar{s})\backslash X_{*}(T_{\bar{s}})
, where
\bar{s}\to S
is a geometric point lying over
s
, defines a locally constant map on
S
which is indeed constant by our above assumption. Its value is
W_{G}\backslash X_{*}
.

Definition 3.14
.

Now let
{\mathscr{E}}
be a
G
-bundle on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S}
. Then its isomorphism class defines a locally constant map

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle\type({\mathscr{E}})\colon S

\displaystyle\longrightarrow W_{G}\backslash X_{*},

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\longmapsto{\mathscr{E}}_{\bar{s}}\in\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})(\bar{s})={\mathcal{C}}_{G}(\bar{s})=W_{G}\backslash X_{*},

called the
type of
{\mathscr{E}}
.

Let
S^{\prime}\to S
be an étale covering such that
{\mathcal{C}}_{G}\times_{S}S^{\prime}
is constant, for instance an étale covering that splits
G
. Then the type morphism yields a decomposition of the stack of types of
G
-bundles into open and closed substacks

(3.2.5)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})_{S^{\prime}}=\coprod_{[\mu]\in W_{G}\backslash X_{*}}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}).

By Proposition
3.10
,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S^{\prime}})
is a smooth gerbe over
S^{\prime}
which is étale locally isomorphic to
B\Cent_{G}(\mu)
where
\mu
is some cocharacter of
G
representing
[\mu]\in W_{G}\backslash X_{*}
.

Remark 3.15
.

Let
S=\Spec O
for a normal local ring
O
with field of fractions
K
. Then any reductive group
G
over
O
is split over a finite étale extension
O^{\prime}
which we may assume to be the integral closure of
O
in an
O
-unramified extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
. Let
K
be a separable closure of
K
and let
K^{\textup{ur}}
be the maximal
O
-unramified extension of
K
in
K^{\sep}
. If
\bar{\eta}
denotes the geometric point of
\Spec O
given by
K^{\sep}
, then we find
\Gal(K^{\textup{ur}}/K)\cong\pi_{1}(\Spec O,\bar{\eta})
.

Hence every conjugacy class
[\mu]
is defined over the integral closure
O^{\textup{ur}}
of
O
in
K^{\textup{ur}}
and has a ring of definition which is a finite étale
O
-algebra contained in
O^{\textup{ur}}
. we call this ring of definition
O_{[\mu]}
. Then
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is defined over
O_{[\mu]}
.

In general, there exists no cocharacter of
G
representing
\mu
over
O_{[\mu]}
. But after passage to a finite étale extension
O^{\prime}
of
O_{[\mu]}
,
[\mu]
can be represented by a cocharacter
\mu
of
G_{O^{\prime}}
. Then
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})_{O^{\prime}}\cong B\Cent_{G_{O^{\prime}}}(\mu)
. If
G_{O_{[\mu]}}
is quasi-split (e.g.,
O
is henselian with finite residue field), one can choose
O^{\prime}=O_{[\mu]}
.

Example 3.16
.

Consider the special case
G=\GL_{n}
. Then we can identify

W_{G}\backslash X_{*}=\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{+}:=\{\,(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\ ;\ \mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{n}\,\}.

Let
S
be a scheme. A
\GL_{n}
-bundle over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,S}
is the same as a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{E}}_{i}
of rank
n
on
S
. Then its type is constant if and only if
\rk({\mathscr{E}}_{i})
is constant for all
i
. In this case,
{\mathscr{E}}
has type
\mu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})
if

\rk({\mathscr{E}}_{i})=n_{i}:=\#\{\,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}\ ;\ \mu_{j}=i\,\}.

Then only finitely many of the
n_{i}
are nonzero and
n=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}n_{i}
. Since
G
is a split reductive group, every conjugacy class of cocharacters is already defined over
S
and even has a representative over
S
. We have

\Bun_{G}^{\mu}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,S})\cong B\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\GL_{n_{i},S}.

Example 3.17
.

Let
\kappa
be a field and let
T
be a torus over
\kappa
. Suppose for simplicity that there exists a quadratic Galois extension
\kappa^{\prime}/\kappa
that splits
T
and set
\Gamma:=\Gal(\kappa^{\prime}/\kappa)=\{\id,\sigma\}
. Then the locally constant group scheme
\underline{X}_{*}(T)
becomes constant after base change to
\kappa^{\prime}
, and
\underline{X}_{*}(T)
is given by the free abelian group
X_{*}(T)
with its
\Gamma
-action. Since
T
is abelian,
W_{T}=1
and the action of
T
on
\underline{X}_{*}(T)
is trivial.

For a cocharacter
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
there are now two cases.

(1)

If
\sigma(\mu)=\mu
, then
\mu
defines a
\kappa
-rational point of
\underline{X}_{*}(T)
and
\Bun_{G}^{\mu}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa})=BT
.

(2)

If
\sigma(\mu)\neq\mu
, then a field of definition of
\mu
is
\kappa^{\prime}
and
\Bun_{G}^{\mu}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa^{\prime}})\cong BT\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
.

3.3.
The display group attached to a functor of graded
R
-algebras

In this section, we axiomatize and generalize results of Lau from
[
Lau21
, 5,6]
. In particular, we define a more general version of the so-called display group of loc. cit., which we also call the display group even though we do not use the notion of a display. Although the results in this section are much more general than in loc. cit., most proofs are heavily inspired by Lau’s proofs.

We work in the following set-up. Let
O\to\kappa
be a surjective map of rings. Let
R\mapsto{\mathscr{B}}_{R}
be a functor from the category of
\kappa
-algebras to the category of quasi-coherent algebras over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
. We set
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}:=\Spec{\mathscr{B}}_{R}
and denote by
{\mathcal{X}}
the functor
R\mapsto{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
from the category of
\kappa
-algebras to the category of relatively affine schemes over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
. For every
\kappa
-algebra
R
one has a cartesian diagram

\textstyle{X_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec O\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,O},}

where
X_{R}=\Spec B_{R}
is the spectrum of the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
O
-algebra
B_{R}
corresponding to
{\mathscr{B}}_{R}
, and
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}=[\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash X_{R}]
.

Let
G
be a smooth affine group scheme over
O
, let
[\mu]
be
G
-conjugacy class of coharacters of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
defined over
O
, let
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
be the corresponding
G
-bundle oover
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
and let
[\mu]\backslash G
the associated algebraic group quotient stack over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
. Ty simplify the exposition, we assume that
[\mu]
can be represented by a cocharacter
\mu
of
G
over
O
.

For every
\kappa
-algebra
R
we set

(3.3.1)

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,\mu)(R):=E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])(R):=\Hom_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}}({\mathcal{X}}_{R},[\mu]\backslash G).

Then
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])(R)
is a group (and not a group objects in groupoids) since we can identify the underlying groupoids via our choice of
\mu
as follows

(3.3.2)

\displaystyle E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])(R)

\displaystyle=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X_{R},G):=\{\text{$\mathbb{G}_{m}$-equivariant morphisms $X_{R}\to G$}\}

\displaystyle=\{\text{degree $0$ maps $\Gamma(G,{\mathscr{O}}_{G})\to B_{R}$ of $\mathbb{Z}$-graded $O$-algebras}\}

by Remark
1.3
, and the right-hand side is a set. Hence we obtain a presheaf of groups on the category of
\kappa
-algebras.

Definition 3.18
.

The presheaf of groups on
\kappa
-algebras
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])
is called the
(G,[\mu])
-display group of
{\mathcal{X}}
.

In all cases of interest for us, we will be able to apply Proposition
3.24
below to show that
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])
is representable by an affine group scheme.

Remark 3.19
.

Let
G^{\prime}
be a second smooth affine group scheme over
O
, let
\varphi\colon G\to G^{\prime}
be a map of group schemes and let
[\mu^{\prime}]=[\varphi\circ\mu]
be the
G^{\prime}
-conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G^{\prime}
obtained as the image of
[\mu]
under
\varphi
. Then
\varphi
induces a map of algebraic stacks
[\mu]\backslash G\to[\mu^{\prime}]\backslash G^{\prime}
and hence by composition a map of presheaves of groups

(3.3.3)

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])\longrightarrow E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G^{\prime},[\mu^{\prime}]).

If
G\to G^{\prime}
is a monomorphism, the description (
3.3.2
) shows that (
3.3.3
) is a monomorphism.

Next we will give an alternative description of
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])
.

Remark and Definition 3.20
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
be the structure map and denote by
{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])
the pullback of
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
(Remark
3.9
) to
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}
. It is called the
standard
G
-bundle of type
[\mu]
for
{\mathcal{X}}
.

We denote by
\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu]))
the functor that sends a
\kappa
-algebra
R
to the group of automorphisms of the
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])_{R}
over
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
, where
{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])_{R}
denotes the pullback of
{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])
under
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}
.

Since an automorphism of
{\mathscr{E}}(G,[\mu])_{R}
is given by a point
g\in G(X_{R})=\Hom(X_{R},G)
that is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, we obtain the following alternative description of the display group.

Proposition 3.21
.

There is a functorial isomorphism of presheaves of groups on the category of
\kappa
-algebras

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])\overset{\sim}{\to}\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])).

Example 3.22
.

Let
\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,O}\to G
be a cocharacter.

(1)

Let
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}=B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
, viewed as a stack over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
via
O\to\kappa\to R
. Then
B_{R}=R
with the trivial
\mathbb{Z}
-grading and we find

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(\mu)(R)=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(*,G)=\Cent_{G}(\mu)(R)

Hence
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(\mu)=\Cent_{G}(\mu)
.

(2)

Let
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R})^{\pm}]
, i.e.,
B_{R}=R[t]
endowed with the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading such that
\deg(t)=\pm 1
. By (
3.3.2
) we find

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(\mu)(R)=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\mathbb{A}_{R}^{1})^{\pm},G)=P^{\pm}(\mu)(R).

Hence
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(\mu)=P^{\pm}(\mu)
.

Example 3.23
.

Suppose
\mu
is the trivial cocharacter. Then

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,\mu)(R)=\Hom_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}}({\mathcal{X}}_{R},B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}\times G)=\Hom_{O}({\mathcal{X}}_{R},G)=G((B_{R})_{0}),

where the last equality holds since
\Spec(B_{R})_{0}
is a good moduli space for
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
and therefore
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}\to\Spec(B_{R})_{0}
is initial in the category of morphisms from
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
to algebraic spaces.

In Example
3.22
,
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(\mu)
is representable by a group scheme. This fact is a special case of the following general principle.

Proposition 3.24
.

In the situation introduced in the beginning of this section
suppose that
R\mapsto(B_{R})_{i}
is representable for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
by a scheme
on the category of
\kappa
-algebras.

Let
Y=\Spec C
be an affine
O
-scheme of finite type with
\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
-action and set
{\mathcal{Y}}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,O}\backslash Y]
. Then the set valued functor on
\kappa
-algebras

(3.3.4)

E({\mathcal{Y}})\colon R\mapsto\Hom_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}}({\mathcal{X}}_{R},{\mathcal{Y}})

is representable by a scheme.

(1)

If
R\mapsto(B_{R})_{i}
is representable for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
by an affine scheme (resp. by an affine
\kappa
-scheme of finite type), then (
3.3.4
) is representable by an affine scheme (resp. by an affine scheme of finite type).

(2)

Suppose that the following conditions are satisfied.

(a)

The functor
R\mapsto(B_{R})_{i}
is representable for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
by a smooth affine scheme
Z_{i}
of finite type.

(b)

Y
is smooth over
O
.

(c)

If
S\to R
is a surjective map of
\kappa
-algebras with square zero kernel, then
B_{S}\to B_{R}
has nilpotent kernel (note that
B_{S}\to B_{R}
is surjective by
(a)
).

Then (
3.3.4
) is representable by a smooth affine scheme.

This proposition axiomatizes and generalizes
[
Lau21
, 5.3.1, 5.3.8]
but the proof is essentially the same as in loc. cit.

Proof.

First assume that
Y=(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{O})^{(i)}
. Then

E({\mathcal{Y}})(R)=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(X_{R},Y)=(B_{R})_{i}

and all assertions hold by assumption. Therefore the same holds also if
Y
is a product
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{O}
, where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on every coordinate with possibly different weights. In general,
Y
is the equalizer of a pair of maps
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{O}\rightrightarrows\mathbb{A}^{m}_{O}
where each coordinate is homegeneous of some degree. This shows all assertions except the last one about smoothness of
E({\mathcal{Y}})
which is proved as in the second half of the proof of
[
Lau21
, 5.3.8]
.
∎

Example 3.25
.

Let
R\mapsto A_{R}
be a functor from the category of
\kappa
-algebras to the category of
O
-algebras and define
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}:=\Spec A_{R}
with structure map
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}\to\Spec O\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
. By (
3.3.2
) one has for every
\kappa
-algebra
R

E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,\mu)(R)=\Hom^{\mathbb{G}_{m,A_{R}}}(\mathbb{G}_{m,A_{R}},G_{A_{R}})=G(A_{R}),

where the last identity is induced by evaluation in
1
.

If
R\mapsto A_{R}
is representable by an affine scheme, then
E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(\mu)
is representable by an affine group scheme by Proposition
3.24
.

Remark 3.26
.

Let
R\mapsto{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
and
R\mapsto\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
be two functors as above and let
\alpha_{R}\colon{\mathcal{X}}_{R}\to\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
be maps of stacks over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
functorial in
R
. By composition, the
\alpha_{R}
induce a map of presheaves of groups

E_{\alpha}\colon E_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])\longrightarrow E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu]).

Proposition 3.27
.

With the notation of Remark
3.26
, suppose that
\alpha_{R}
is a closed immersion and that
((\tilde{B}_{R})_{0},\Ker((\alpha^{*}_{R})_{0}))
is a henselian pair for every
\kappa
-algebra
R
. Then the induced map

E_{\alpha}\colon E_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])(R)\longrightarrow E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])(R)

is surjective for all
R
.

Proof.

The adequate moduli spaces (in this case even good moduli spaces) of
{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
(in the sense of
[
Alp14
, 5.1.1]
) are
\Spec(B_{R})_{0}
and
\Spec(\tilde{B}_{R})_{0}
, respectively, by
[
Alp14
, 9.1.4]
. The hypothesis implies that the pair of algebraic stacks
(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{R},{\mathcal{X}}_{R})
is henselian by
[
AHR23
, 3.6]
and
[\mu\backslash G]
is smooth. Hence we conclude by
[
AHR23
, 7.9]
.
∎

Example 3.28
.

Suppose that
\alpha
in Remark
3.26
is an isomorphism in degree
\leq 0
and that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
G
is repelling (i.e., the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading on
\Gamma(G,{\mathscr{O}}_{G})
is concentrated in degree
\leq 0
). Then by (
3.3.2
),
E_{\alpha}
induces an isomorphism

E_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}E_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu]).

3.4.
Display gerbes

We now change our setting from Section
3.3
slightly. Again we are given a surjective ring homomorphism
O\to\kappa
. Suppose that we are given a functor
R\mapsto({\mathcal{X}}_{R},\alpha_{R})
from the category of
\kappa
-algebras to the category of pairs
({\mathcal{X}},\alpha)
consisting of an algebraic stack
{\mathcal{X}}
over
B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
such that
{\mathcal{X}}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}
is affine. Let
B
be the corresponding
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
O
-algebra (Section
1.1
) and let
\alpha
be an isomorphism of
O
-algebras
\alpha\colon B_{0}/\sum_{i>0}B_{i}B_{-i}\overset{\sim}{\to}R
.

As before, let
G
be a smooth affine group scheme over
O
and let
[\mu]
be a conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G
defined over
O
. We define a stack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\mathcal{X}})
over
\kappa
as the fppf-stackification of the prestack given by

R\mapsto\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{X}}_{R}),\qquad\qquad\text{$R$ a $\kappa$-algebra}.

Via
\alpha_{R}
we can identify
B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}={\mathcal{X}}_{R}^{0}
and pullback via the closed immersion
B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{R}
yields a morphism of stacks

(3.4.1)

t\colon\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\mathcal{X}})\to\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}).

The preimage of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
under
t
is denoted by
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}({\mathcal{X}})
.

Theorem 3.29
.

Suppose that the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded algebra
B_{R}
is henselian (Definition
3.1
) for all
R
. Then one has
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{X}})\cong BE_{{\mathcal{X}}}(G,[\mu])
.

Proof.

The restriction of (
3.4.1
) to a map
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}({\mathcal{X}})\to\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is a gerbe for the fppf-topology because
B_{R}
is henselian for all
R
by Theorem
3.2
. By Proposition
3.10
,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is a gerbe over
\kappa
. Therefore
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{X}})
is a gerbe over
\kappa
. Hence it suffices to find a
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
of type
[\mu]
on
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}
such that
\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}})\cong E_{{\mathcal{X}}}([\mu])
. For this we may choose the standard
G
-bundle of type
\mu
over
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}
by Proposition
3.21
.
∎

4.
Frames and frame stacks

4.1.
Frames

Definition 4.1
.

Let
A
be a ring and let
{\mathscr{B}}
be a filtered
A
-algebra, given by
(B_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B_{j})
(Definition
2.6
), let
B_{-\infty}
be its underlying ring, and let
\Re(B)
be the associated Rees stack.

(1)

Let
\sigma
be a morphism

\sigma\colon\Spec B_{-\infty}\longrightarrow\Re(B).

We will call such a pair
((B_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B_{j}),\sigma)
a
quasi-frame
. The composition

\Spec B_{0}\to\Spec B_{-\infty}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\sigma}{\longrightarrow}}}\Re(B)\longrightarrow\Spec B_{0}

corresponds to a ring endomorphism of
B_{0}
, called the
endomorphism of
B_{0}
induced by
\sigma
, which we always denote by
\phi
.

(2)

A quasi-frame
((B_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B_{j}),\sigma)
is called
frame
if the following conditions are satisfied.

(a)

The generalized filtered
A
-algebra
(B_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B_{j})
is a filtered ring
(A,(\Fil^{i}_{A}))
.

(b)

The map
\sigma\colon\Spec B_{\infty}=\Spec A\to\Re(A,(\Fil^{i}_{A}))
factors through the open substack
\Re(A,(\Fil^{i}_{A}))^{\neq-}
.

Let us make more concrete how to define a map
\sigma\colon\Spec A\to\Re(A)^{\neq-}
for a filtered ring as above.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
(A,(\Fil^{i}))
be a filtered ring and let
\phi\colon A\to A
be a ring endomorphism. Then there is a bijection between the set of maps
\sigma\colon\Spec A\longrightarrow\Re(A)^{\neq-}
inducing
\phi
and pairs
(L,\sigma^{*})
, where
L\in\Pic(A)
and where
\sigma^{*}
is a map of graded
A
-algebras

\sigma^{*}\colon\phi^{*}\Rees(A)\to T(L)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}L^{\otimes i}

such the graded ideal generated by the image of

I^{+}\otimes_{A,\phi}A\longrightarrow\phi^{*}\Rees(A)\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\sigma^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}}T(L)

contains
T(L)_{i}=L^{\otimes i}
for some
i
.

Proof.

A map
\sigma
defining a frame and inducing
\phi
is the same as a section of
\Re(A)^{\neq-}\otimes_{A,\phi}A
over
\Spec A
. A section of
\Re(A)\otimes_{A,\phi}A
is given by a map of graded
A
-algebras
\sigma^{*}\colon\phi^{*}\Rees(A)\to T(L):=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}L^{\otimes i}
, where
L
is a line bundle on
A
(Remark
1.1
). The section factors through
\Re(A)^{\neq-}\otimes_{A,\phi}A
if and only if the graded ideal generated by the image of

I^{+}\otimes_{A,\phi}A\longrightarrow\phi^{*}\Rees(A)\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\sigma^{*}}{\longrightarrow}}}T(L)

is all of
T(L)
. Indeed, more generally, if
\varphi\colon A\to B
is a map of rings,
f\colon\Spec B\to\Spec A
the corresponding map of affine schemes, and
I\subseteq A
an ideal, then
f
factors through the open subscheme
\Spec A\setminus V(I)
if and only if
B=\varphi(I)B
.

Now if
J\subseteq T(L)
is any graded ideal such that
J_{i}=T(L)_{i}
, then
J=T(L)
. Indeed, to see that the inclusion
J\to T(L)
is surjective, we can work locally in
\Spec A
. Hence we can assume that
L=A
and hence
T(L)=A[t,t^{-1}]
. Then multiplying by integral powers of
t
shows that
J=A[t,t^{-1}]
. This shows that the graded ideal generated by the image of (*) is
T(L)
if and only if it contains
T(L)_{i}
for some
i
.
∎

Definition and Remark 4.3
.

Let
A
be a ring. A
morphism of quasi-frames over
A

((B_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B_{j}),\sigma_{B})\to((C_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}C_{j}),\sigma_{C})
is a map
\psi\colon(B_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B_{j})\to(C_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}C_{j})
of filtered
A
-algebras (Definition
2.15
) such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\Spec B_{-\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{A}}
\textstyle{\Re(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec C_{-\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{B}}
\textstyle{\Re(C),}

where the vertical maps are induced by
\psi
. We obtain the category of quasi-frames and the full subcategory of frames.

Remark 4.4
.

Our definition of frame generlizes the notion of frame defined by Lau in
[
Lau21
]
.

Remark and Definition 4.5
.

Let
(C,\phi_{C})
be a ring together with an endomorphism. Let
(A,(\Fil_{A}^{i}),\sigma_{A})
be a frame yielding a ring endomorphism
\phi_{A}
of
A
, and let
\psi\colon(A,\phi_{A})\to(C,\phi_{C})
be a ring homomorphism with
\psi\circ\phi_{A}=\phi_{C}\circ\psi
. Set
\Fil^{i}_{C}=\Fil^{i}_{A}\otimes_{A}C
. By Remark
2.14
we have
\Re(C)^{\neq-}=C\otimes_{A}\Re(A)^{\neq-}
and hence we obtain a 2-cartesian diagram

\textstyle{C\otimes_{\phi_{C},C}\Re(C)^{\neq-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes_{\phi_{A},A}\Re(A)^{\neq-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec A.}

Therefore the section
\sigma_{A}
of
A\otimes_{\phi_{A},A}\Re(A)^{\neq-}\to\Spec A
induces by base change along
\psi
a map
\sigma_{C}
that makes
(C,(\Fil^{i}_{C}),\sigma_{C})
into a frame. Moreover
\psi
defines then a morphism of frames
(C,(\Fil^{i}_{C}),\sigma_{C})\to(A,(\Fil_{A}^{i}),\sigma_{A})
.

In particular, we can endow this construction if
C=A/I
for a
\phi_{A}
-invariant ideal. In his case, we call
(C,(\Fil^{i}_{C}),\sigma_{C})
the
quotient frame
of
(A,(\Fil_{A}^{i}),\sigma_{A})
by
I
.

4.2.
The frame stack

Let
(A,(\Fil^{i})_{i},\sigma)
be a frame. By (
2.2.9
) we have the open quasi-compact immersion

(4.2.1)

\tau\colon\Spec A\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Re(A)^{\neq+}\longrightarrow\Re(A)

identifying
\Spec A
with the locus of
\Re(A)
where
t
is invertible.

Definition 4.6
.

The coequalizer

{\mathcal{F}}(A):={\mathcal{F}}(A,(\Fil^{i})_{i},\sigma):=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.1117pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.1117pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Spec A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.86761pt\raise 6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.1117pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.39458pt\raise-6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.1117pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.1117pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Re(A)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

is called the
frame stack
of the frame. Since both
\sigma
and
\tau
factorize both over
\Re(A)^{\neq 0}=\Re(A)^{\neq+}\cup\Re(A)^{\neq-}
, we can also define

{\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0}:={\mathcal{F}}(A,(\Fil^{i})_{i},\sigma)^{\neq 0}:=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.1117pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.1117pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Spec A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.86761pt\raise 6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.1117pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.39458pt\raise-6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.1117pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.1117pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Re(A)^{\neq 0}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

In both cases we take the coequalizer in the 2-category of Adams stacks, see Appendix
A
. Note that
\Re(A)
and
\Re(A)^{\neq 0}
are both Adams stacks by Proposition
A.4
.

Remark 4.7
.

Let
G
be a group scheme over
A
such that
BG
is an Adams stack. It follows that the category of vector bundles (resp. the groupoid of
G
-bundles) on
{\mathcal{F}}(A)
is equivalent to the category (resp. groupoid) of pairs
({\mathscr{E}},\Phi)
consisting of a vector bundle (resp.
G
-bundle)
{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Re(A)
and an isomorphism of vector bundles (resp.
G
-bundles)
\Phi\colon\sigma^{*}{\mathscr{E}}\to\tau^{*}{\mathscr{E}}
over
\Spec A
.

A similar description holds for vector bundles and
G
-bundles over
{\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0}
.

Remark 4.8
.

With the notation above, we have a 2-commutative diagram of Adams stacks

\textstyle{\Spec A\amalg\Spec A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau\amalg\sigma}
can
\textstyle{\Re(A)^{\neq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Re(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{F}}(A),}

where the left square and the combined rectangle are pushout squares by definition. Therefore the right square is a pushout square.

If
{\mathcal{X}}
is any Adams stack, then we obtain a 2-cartesian square of groupoids

\textstyle{\Hom(\Re(A)^{\neq 0},{\mathcal{X}})}
\textstyle{\Hom(\Re(A),{\mathcal{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom({\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0},{\mathcal{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Hom({\mathcal{F}}(A),{\mathcal{X}}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

In particular, if the restriction induces an equivalence (resp. a fully faithful functor)
\Hom(\Re(A),{\mathcal{X}})\to\Hom(\Re(A)^{\neq 0},{\mathcal{X}})
, then it follows formally that composition with
{\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0}\to{\mathcal{F}}(A)
also induces an equivalence (resp. a fully faithful functor)
\Hom({\mathcal{F}}(A),{\mathcal{X}})\to\Hom({\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0},{\mathcal{X}})
. In particular we have the following result.

Lemma 4.9
.

Suppose that
\textup{Vec}(\Re(A))\to\textup{Vec}(\Re(A)^{\neq 0})
is an equivalence (resp. fully faithful). Then
\textup{Vec}({\mathcal{F}}(A))\to\textup{Vec}({\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0})
is an equivalence (resp. fully faithful). Similarly, let
G
be a group scheme over
A
such that
\Bun_{G}(\Re(A))\to\Bun_{G}(\Re(A)^{\neq 0})
is an equivalence (resp. fully faithful). Then
\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A))\to\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A)^{\neq 0})
is an equivalence (resp. fully faithful).

4.3.
Classifying
G
-bundles on frame stacks

We fix the following general setting. Let
O
be a Dedekind domain and let
O\to\kappa
be a surjective homomorphism of rings. Let
A\colon R\mapsto(A_{R},(\Fil_{A_{R}}^{i}),\sigma_{R},\alpha_{R})
be a presheaf on the category of
\kappa
-algebras with values in the category of tuples consisting of a frame over
O
and an isomorphism
\alpha_{R}\colon A_{R}/t(\Fil_{A_{R}}^{1})\overset{\sim}{\to}R
of
O
-algebras.

Let
\Re(A_{R})
be the Rees stack attached to the filtered ring
(A_{R},(\Fil^{i}_{A_{R}}))
. Via the composition

\Re(A_{R})\to A_{R}^{\Fil}\to O^{\Fil}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,O}

we can consider
R\mapsto(\Re(A_{R}),\alpha_{R})
as a functor as described in the beginning of Section
3.4
. In particular we obtain for every smooth affine group scheme
G
and every conjugacy class
[\mu]
of a cocharacter
\mu
of
G
the display group
E_{A}(G,[\mu])
(Section
3.3
). We denote by
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(A)
the prestack
R\mapsto\Bun_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Re(A_{R}))
.

For every prestack
{\mathcal{X}}
over
O
we define a prestack over
\kappa
by

L^{A}{\mathcal{X}}(R):={\mathcal{X}}(A_{R}),\qquad\qquad\text{for every $\kappa$-algebra $R$}.

We make the following assumptions.

Assumption 4.10
.

(a)

The functor
R\mapsto\Fil^{i}_{A_{R}}
is representable for all
i\geq 0
by an affine scheme.

(b)

The pair
(A_{R},t(\Fil^{1}_{A_{R}}))
is henselian for every
\kappa
-algebra
R
.

(c)

The canonical map of stacks
B(L^{A}G)\to L^{A}(BG)
is an equivalence.

Condition
(a)
ensures that the display group
E_{\Re(A)}(G,[\mu])
is representable by an affine group scheme over
\kappa
by Proposition
3.24
. Using Hypothesis
(b)
, Theorem
3.29
yields an equivalence of stacks

(4.3.1)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(A)\cong BE_{A}(G,[\mu]).

Remark 4.11
.

Let us look more closely at the functor of groups
L^{A}G
. As in the proof of Proposition
3.24
, Condition
(a)
implies that
L^{A}G
is representable by an affine group scheme, which is of finite type if
R\mapsto A_{R}
is representable by a
\kappa
-scheme of finite type. Moreover,
L^{A}G
is a smooth group scheme if
R\mapsto A_{R}
is representable by a smooth affine scheme and
A_{S}\to A_{R}
has nilpotent kernel for every surjective map of
\kappa
-algebras
S\to R
with square zero kernel.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}(A_{R})
be the frame stack attached to the frame
(A_{R},(\Fil_{A_{R}}^{i}),\sigma_{R})
. Since
O
is a Dedekind domain,
BG
is an Adams stack by Example
A.5
and hence we have by Remark
4.7

(4.3.2)

\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A_{R}))=\lim(\Bun_{G}(\Re(A_{R}))\rightrightarrows\Bun_{G}(A_{R})),

where the two arrows are given via pullback with
\tau
and
\sigma
. We set

(4.3.3)

\displaystyle\Bun_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{F}}(A_{R}))

\displaystyle:=\lim(\Bun^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Re(A_{R}))\rightrightarrows\Bun_{G}(A_{R})),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{F}}(A))(R)

\displaystyle:=\Bun_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{F}}(A_{R})).

This defines a prestack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{F}}(A))
over
\kappa
.

We now come to our main general classification result. Consider the display group attached to the functor
R\mapsto A_{R}
as in Example
3.25
. It is given by the affine group scheme
L^{A}G
. By Remark
3.26
, the two functorial maps
\tau,\sigma\colon\Spec A_{R}\to\Re(A_{R})
induce maps of affine group schemes over
\kappa

(4.3.4)

E_{A}(G,[\mu])\rightrightarrows L^{A}G,

again denoted by
\tau
and
\sigma
. This defines an action of the group scheme
E_{A}(G,[\mu])
on the
\kappa
-scheme
L^{A}G
and we can form the fpqc quotient
[E_{A}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{A}G]
.

Theorem 4.12
.

There is a functorial equivalence of fpqc-stacks

(4.3.5)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{F}}(A))\cong[E_{A}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{A}G].

Proof.

We have functorial identities

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}({\mathcal{F}}(A))

\displaystyle=\lim(\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(A)\rightrightarrows L^{(N)}L^{A}(BG))

\displaystyle=\lim(BE_{A}(G,[\mu])\rightrightarrows L^{A}(BG))

\displaystyle=\lim(BE_{A}(G,[\mu])\rightrightarrows BL^{A}G)

\displaystyle=[E_{A}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{A}(G)].

The first identity holds by definition, the second by (
4.3.1
), the third identity holds by Assumption
(c)
above, and the last identity follows from Lemma
4.13
below.

The quotient stack
[E_{A}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{A}(G)]
has an affine diagonal and in particular is an fpqc-stack.
∎

In the proof of the theorem we have used the following general lemma.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a site, let
E
and
H
be sheaves of groups on
{\mathcal{C}}
, and let
\tau,\sigma\colon E\to H
be homomorphisms of sheaves of groups. Denote
{\bar{\tau}},{\bar{\sigma}}\colon BE\to BH
be the induced maps on classifying spaces. Define a left action of
E
one the underlying sheaf of sets of
H
by

E\times H\longrightarrow H,\qquad(e,h)\mapsto\tau(e)h\sigma(e^{-1}).

Then one has a functorial equivalence of stacks

\lim(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 11.02255pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.02255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{BE\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.81421pt\raise 7.99165pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\bar{\tau}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.02255pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.81421pt\raise-7.99165pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\bar{\sigma}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.02255pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.02255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{BH}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)\cong[E\backslash H].

Proof.

It suffices to show the equivalence for the corresponding prestacks and then stackify, i.e., we may assume that
{\mathcal{C}}
is endowed with the trivial topology (where only isomorphisms define sieves). In this case this a straight forward calculation:

For
R\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the objects of
\lim(BE\rightrightarrows BH)(R)
are given by pairs
(\varepsilon,h)
, where
\varepsilon\in\Ob(BE(R))=\{*\}
and
h\in\Mor(BH(R))=H(R)
is an isomorphism
{\bar{\sigma}}(\varepsilon)\overset{\sim}{\to}{\bar{\tau}}(\varepsilon)
. Hence the set of objects are the elements of
H(R)
. The morphisms
h\to h^{\prime}
are isomorphisms in
BE(R)
, i.e., elements of
E(R)
, such that
\tau(e)h=h^{\prime}\sigma(e)
. But this is the description of the prestack
[H\backslash E]
.
∎

4.4.
Colimits of frame stacks

In this section, we denote by
(A_{N},(\Fil_{A_{N}}^{i}),\sigma_{N})_{N\geq 1}
be an
\mathbb{N}^{\opp}
-diagram of frames. We suppose that the following hypotheses are satisfied.

(1)

The maps
\Fil_{A_{N+1}}^{i}\to\Fil_{A_{N}}^{i}
are surjective for all
N\geq 1
and all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, in particular
A_{N+1}\to A_{N}
is surjective for all
N
.

(2)

The kernel of
A_{N+1}\to A_{N}
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
A_{N+1}
for all
N\geq 1
.

(3)

The
A_{N}
-modules
\Fil^{i}_{A_{N}}
are finite projective for all
i
.

We obtain closed immersions of the attached Rees stacks
\Re(A_{N})\to\Re(A_{N+1})
for all
N\geq 1
. We set

A:=\lim_{N}A_{N},\qquad\Fil_{A}^{i}:=\lim_{N}\Fil_{A_{N}}^{i}

and obtain a filtered ring
(A,(\Fil^{i}_{A}))
with attached Rees stack
\Re(A)
. By Proposition
1.9
, applied to
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A[t]
-modules, we obtain an equivalence of Adams stacks

(4.4.1)

\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N}\Re(A_{N})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Re(A).

Since we also have
\Spec A=\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N}\Spec A_{N}
by Example
1.8
, we also obtain formally

(4.4.2)

\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N}{\mathcal{F}}(A_{N})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{F}}(A)

since coequalizers commute with colimits.

5.
G
-bundles over the Rees stack attached to the
v
-adic filtration

In this chapter, we will study a type of Rees stack that will be used for (truncated) local shtukas and for prisms. Throughout the whole section let
A
be a ring, let
L
be an invertible
A
-module, and let
v\colon L\to A
be an
A
-linear map. The pair
(L,v)
corresponds to a section of
A^{\Fil}\to\Spec A
which is also denoted by
v\colon\Spec A\to A^{\Fil}
.

Define a filtration on
A
by
\Fil^{j}:=L^{\otimes j}
for
j\geq 0
and
\Fil^{j}:=A
for
j<0
and define
t\colon\Fil^{j+1}\to\Fil^{j}
to be the multiplication with
v
for
j\geq 0
and the identity for
j<0
. We obtain a filtered ring
(A,(L^{\otimes j})_{j})
with associated
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A[t]
-algebra

B:=\Rees(A,v):=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Fil^{j}

and we denote by

\Re(A,v):=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec B]

the attached Rees stack.

Remark 5.1
.

There are the following two special cases.

(1)

If
v
is injective, then
L
is identified via
v
with an invertible ideal
I
of
A
and we also write
\Re(A,I)
instead of
\Re(A,v)
.

(2)

If
L=A
, then
v
is the multiplication with an element
z\in A
and we also write
\Re(A,z)
instead of
\Re(A,v)
. In this case we set
u:=1\in\Fil^{1}
. Then
tu=z
and we obtain a presentation of the underlying
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A
-algebra of
B
as

(5.0.1)

B=A[t,u]/(tu-z),\qquad\deg(t)=-1,\ \deg(u)=1.

Zariski locally on
A
, we can always choose an isomorphism
L\cong A
, i.e., Zariski locally on
A
, the
A[t]
-algebra
B
is isomorphic to
A[t,u]/(tu-z)
for some
z\in A
.

5.1.
The Rees stack attached to the
v
-adic filtration

Let us describe the attractor, reppeller, and the fixed point locus of
\Re(A,v)
.

Remark 5.2
.

By Remark
2.13
we have

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle R:=B^{0}

\displaystyle=A/v(L),

\displaystyle B^{-}

\displaystyle=R[t],

\displaystyle B^{+}

\displaystyle=B/tB=\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}L^{\otimes j}\otimes_{A}R=\Sym_{R}(L\otimes_{A}R).

Hence the cartesian diagram (
2.2.8
) has the form

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 42.53075pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 66.53075pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Re(A,v)^{+}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash\Spec\Sym_{R}(L\otimes_{A}R)]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 267.01476pt\raise-27.55501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-42.53075pt\raise-32.55113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Re(A,v)^{0}=B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.01671pt\raise-8.3611pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 117.51488pt\raise-58.29405pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 151.77275pt\raise-32.55113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 267.01476pt\raise-32.55113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Re(A,v).}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-66.40782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 117.51488pt\raise-66.40782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Re(A,v)^{-}=R^{\Fil}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 267.01476pt\raise-37.76707pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

The composition
\Re(A,v)^{-}=R^{\Fil}\to\Re(A,v)
with the Rees map
\Re(A,v)\to A^{\Fil}
is the natural closed embedding
R^{\Fil}\to A^{\Fil}
.

We have already seen in (
2.2.9
) that the inclusion
\Re(A,v)^{\neq+}\to\Re(A,v)
can be identified with

\tau\colon\Re(A,v)^{\neq+}\cong\Spec A\longrightarrow\Re(A,v).

Since
B^{-}=B/I^{+}
and
I^{+}
is the ideal generated by
L=B_{1}
, we find
\Re(A,v)^{\neq-}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash B^{\neq-}]
with
B^{\neq-}=B[L^{-1}]=T(L)
(Remark
1.1
) which shows that

\Re(A,v)^{\neq-}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash T(L)]=\Spec A.

Hence we find that

\Re(A,v)^{\neq-}\cap\Re(A,v)^{\neq+}=\Spec A[1/v],

with
A[1/v]
as defined in Example
2.11
. It follows that the algebraic stack

\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}=\Re(A,v)^{\neq-}\cup\Re(A,v)^{\neq+}

is the scheme obtained by gluing two copies of
\Spec A
along
\Spec A[1/v]
.

Let us collect some properties of the scheme
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
.

Remark 5.3
.

(1)

The scheme
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
is separated if and only if
\Spec A[1/v]
is also a closed subscheme of
\Spec A
, for instance if
v
is nilpotent and hence
A[1/v]=0
.

(2)

As the inclusion
\Spec A[1/v]\to\Spec A
is affine,
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
always has an affine diagonal.

(3)

Let
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
be the two copies of
\Spec A
in
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
. For
n\in\mathbb{Z}
define a line bundle
{\mathscr{L}}_{n}
on
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
by gluing the line bundle defined by
L
on
U_{1}
and the the trivial line bundle on
U_{2}
along
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\Spec A[1/v]
by the isomorphism
v^{n}
. We claim that
({\mathscr{L}}_{1},{\mathscr{L}}_{-1})
is an ample pair of line bundles.

Indeed, global sections of
{\mathscr{L}}_{n}
are pairs
(b,a)
with
b\in L^{\otimes n}
and
a\in A
such that
v^{n}(b)=a
in
A[1/v]
. Choose
s_{1},\dots,s_{r}\in L
such that the non-vanishing loci of the
s_{i}
are affine and cover
\Spec A
. Then the non-vanishing loci of the sections
(s_{i},v(s_{i}))
of
{\mathscr{L}}_{1}
are the open affine subscheme
(U_{1})_{s_{i}}
, and these subschemes cover
U_{1}
. The non-vanishing locus of the global section
(v(1),1)
of
{\mathscr{L}}_{-1}
is
U_{2}
. Hence
({\mathscr{L}}_{1},{\mathscr{L}}_{-1})
is an ample pair.

(4)

In particular,
\Re(A)^{\neq 0}
has the resolution property, i.e. every finite type quasi-coherent module over
\Re(A)^{\neq 0}
is the quotient of a vector bundle, even of a finite direct sum of tensor powers of
{\mathscr{L}}_{-1}
and
{\mathscr{L}}_{1}
. Hence
\Re(A)^{\neq 0}
is an Adams stack.

The construction of
\Re(A,v)
is functorial in
(A,v)
as follows.

Remark 5.4
.

Let
A\to A^{\prime}
be a map of rings, let
L^{\prime}
be an invertible
A^{\prime}
-module and
v^{\prime}\colon L^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}
be an
A^{\prime}
-linear map. Suppose we are given a ring-homomorphism
f\colon A\to A^{\prime}
and an
A
-linear map
u\colon L\to L^{\prime}
such that
v^{\prime}\circ u=f\circ v
. Then we obtain an induced map of algebraic stacks

\Re(A,v)\longrightarrow\Re(A^{\prime},v^{\prime}).

Remark 5.5
.

Let
A
and
v\colon L\to A
be as above. Then the category of filtered modules over
(A,(L^{\otimes j})_{j\geq 0})
, i.e. of quasi-coherent modules over
\Re(A,v)
, is equivalent to the category of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A
-modules
M=\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}
together with
A
-linear maps

t\colon M_{j+1}\to M_{j},\qquad u\colon M_{j}\otimes_{A}L\to M_{j+1}

such that
t\circ u
is the multiplication with
v
and
(u\otimes\id_{L^{\otimes-1}})\circ t\colon M_{j+1}\to M_{j+1}\otimes L^{\otimes-1}
is the multiplication with
v{}^{\vee}\colon A\to L{}^{\vee}=L^{\otimes-1}
.

For instance, for the free module
B
of rank
1
, the maps
u\colon\Fil^{j}\otimes L\to\Fil^{j+1}
are given by
v
for
j<0
and by
\id_{L^{\otimes(j+1)}}
for
j\geq 0
.

If
L=A
and hence
v
is given by multiplying with an element
z\in A
, then quasi-coherent modules over
\Re(A,z)=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec A[t,u]/(tu-z)]
are graded
A
-modules
M=\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}
together with
A
-linear maps
t\colon M_{j+1}\to M_{j}
and
u\colon M_{j}\to M_{j+1}
such that
t\circ u
and
u\circ t
is the multiplication with
z
.

If
L=A
and
z=p
is a prime number, then a quasi-coherent module over
\Re(A,p)
is nothing but a
p
-gauge in the sense of Fontaine and Jannsen
[
FJ21
]
.

Remark 5.6
.

As before let
R:=A/v(L)
and set
L_{R}:=L\otimes_{A}R
. Let us describe the pullbacks of filtered modules over
(A,(L^{\otimes j}))
given by
M=\bigoplus M_{j}
as in Remark
5.5
to the various substacks. Since later on we are only interested in pullbacks of vector bundles, we describe only the non-derived pullback.

(1)

The pullback to the closed substack
\Re(A,v)^{+}=\Sym_{R}(L_{R})
is given by the graded
R
-module
M/tM=\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}/t(M_{j+1})
together with
R
-linear maps
u\colon M_{j-1}/tM_{j}\otimes_{R}L_{R}\to M_{j}/t(M_{j+1})
(cf. Example
1.2
).

The pullback to
\Re(A,z)^{-}=R^{\Fil}
is
M/u(M\otimes L)=\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}/u(M_{j-1}\otimes_{A}L)
together with
R
-linear maps
t\colon M_{j+1}/u(M_{j}\otimes L)\to M_{j}/u(M_{j-1}\otimes L)
.

The pullback to the closed substack
\Re(A,z)^{0}=B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}=\Re(A,v)^{+}\cap\Re(A,v)^{-}
is given by the graded
R
-module
\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}/(u(M_{j-1}\otimes L)+t(M_{j+1}))
.

(2)

The pullback to the open substack
\Re(A,v)^{\neq+}=\Spec A
is

M_{-\infty}:=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\cdots\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}M_{j+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}M_{j}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}M_{j-1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}\cdots).

The pullback to
\Re(A,v)^{\neq-}=\Spec A
is

M_{\infty}:=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\cdots\mathrel{\smash{\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}}}M_{j-1}\otimes L^{\otimes(1-j)}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}}}M_{j}\otimes L^{\otimes-j}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}}}M_{j+1}\otimes L^{-j-1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}}}\cdots).

Since the open substack
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
is the scheme obtained by gluing two copies of
\Spec A
along
\Spec A[1/v]
, quasi-coherent modules over
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
are given by triples
(E,E^{\prime},\Phi)
, where
E
and
E^{\prime}
are
A
-modules and where
\Phi
is an isomorphism
E[1/v]\overset{\sim}{\to}E^{\prime}[1/v]
. Then the pullback of
M
to
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
is given by the triple
(M_{-\infty},M_{\infty},\Phi)
, where
\Phi\colon M_{-\infty}[1/v]\overset{\sim}{\to}M_{\infty}[1/v]
is the canonical isomorphism induced by all isomorphisms
M_{j}[1/v]\overset{\sim}{\to}M_{j+1}[1/v]
.

Example 5.7
.

Set
{\mathcal{X}}:=\Re(A,v)
. Then the structure sheaf
{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
corresponds via Remark
5.5
to the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A[t]
-module
B=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Fil^{j}
. The map
u\colon\Fil^{j}\otimes_{A}L\to\Fil^{j+1}
is the multiplication with
v
for
j<0
and the identity of
L^{j+1}
for
j\geq 0
.

More generally, let
{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(e)
be the twisted line bundle for
e\in\mathbb{Z}
(Section
1.3
). It corresponds to
M=\bigoplus M_{j}
with
M_{j}=\Fil^{j+e}
.

Its restriction to
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
is given by
M_{-\infty}=A
,
M_{\infty}=L^{\otimes e}
and
\Phi\colon A[1/v]\to L^{\otimes e}[1/v]
the multiplication with
v^{-e}
.

We can also give the following description of vector bundles on
\Re(A,v)
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
A
,
v\colon L\to A
, and
R=A/v(L)
as above with attached Rees algebra
B=\Rees(A,v)
and attached Rees stack
\Re(A,v)
. Let
{\mathscr{M}}
be a quasi-coherent module over
\Re(A,v)
corresponding to
M=\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}
as in Remark
5.5
. Consider the following conditions on
M
.

(a)

The
A
-modules
M_{j}
are of finite presentation for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and the maps
t\colon M_{j}\to M_{j-1}
are isomorphisms for
j\ll 0
, and the maps
u\colon M_{j-1}\otimes L\to M_{j}
are isomorphisms for
j\gg 0
.

(b)

The maps
t\colon M_{j+1}/u(M_{j}\otimes L)\to M_{j}/u(M_{j-1}\otimes L)
and
u\colon M_{j-1}/tM_{j}\otimes_{R}L_{R}\to M_{j}/t(M_{j+1})
are injective maps of finite projective
R
-modules with projective cokernels for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(c)

The
A
-modules
M_{j}
are finite projective for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(d)

The maps
t\colon M_{j}\to M_{j-1}
and
u\colon M_{j-1}\otimes L\to M_{j}
are injective for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Then one has the following assertions.

(1)

M
defines a module of finite presentation over
\Re(A,v)
if and only if
(a)
holds.

(2)

Suppose that
M
defines a vector bundle over
\Re(A,v)
. Then
(b)
,
(a)
, and
(c)
hold. If
v
is injective, then also
(d)
holds.

(3)

Suppose that
v
is injective. Then the following assertions are equivalent.

(i)

M
defines a vector bundle over
\Re(A,v)
.

(ii)

All conditions
(a)
–
(d)
hold.

(iii)

Condition
(a)
holds, the maps
u\colon M_{j-1}/tM_{j}\otimes_{R}L_{R}\to M_{j}/t(M_{j+1})
are injective maps of finite projective
R
-modules with projective cokernels for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
t\colon M_{j}\to M_{j-1}
is injective for all
j
, and
M_{-\infty}
is a finite projective
A
-module.

(iv)

Condition
(a)
holds, the maps
t\colon M_{j+1}/u(M_{j}\otimes L)\to M_{j}/u(M_{j-1}\otimes L)
are injective maps of finite projective
R
-modules with projective cokernels for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
u\colon M_{j}\otimes L\to M_{j+1}
is injective for all
j
, and
M_{\infty}
is a finite projective
A
-module.

(v)

Conditions
(a)
,
(b)
, and
(c)
hold, and
M_{-\infty}[1/v]
(isomorphic to
M_{\infty}[1/v]
) is a finite projective
A[1/v]
-module.

(4)

Suppose that
v(L)
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
A
. Then
M
defines a vector bundle over
\Re(A,z)
if and only if Conditions
(a)
,
(b)
, and
(c)
hold.

(5)

Suppose that
v=0
, then
M
defines a vector bundle over
\Re(A,v)
if and only if
(b)
holds.

Proof.

All assertions and conditions are Zariski locally on
A
, therefore we may assume
L=A
and hence
B=A[t,u]/(tu-z)
with
\deg(t)=-1
,
\deg(u)=1
and
z\in A
by Remark
5.1
. In this case
v
is injective if and only if
z=v(1)
is regular in
A
.

Let us show
(1)
. Since
M
is of finite presentation, it is the cokernel of a map of graded
B
-modules
F^{\prime}\to F
such that both
F^{\prime}
and
F
are finite graded free. Since
(a)
holds for
B(e)
, we deduce that it holds for
M
.
Conversely, suppose that
(a)
holds. Let
a\leq b
be integers such that
t\colon M_{j}\to M_{j-1}
is an isomorphism for
j\leq a
and such that
u\colon M_{j}\to M_{j+1}
is an isomorphism for
j\geq b
. If
S
is a homogeneous generating system for the
A
-module
M^{a}\oplus M^{a+1}\oplus\cdots\oplus M^{b}
, then
S
is a generating system for the graded
B
-module
M
. It defines a surjection from a finite graded free module
F\to M
. Its kernel
N
is a graded
B
-module such that still the maps
t\colon N_{j}\to N_{j-1}
are isomorphisms for
j\ll 0
, and the maps
u\colon N_{j-1}\to N_{j}
are isomorphisms for
j\gg 0
. Since
M_{j}
is of finite presentation,
N_{j}
is a finitely generated
A
-module. Now the same argument as before shows that we again find a surjection from a finite graded free module
F^{\prime}\to N
. Therefore, we have constructed a finite presentation
F^{\prime}\to F\to M\to 0
by finite graded free modules. Hence
M
is of finite presentation.

Next we show
(2)
. Let
M
define a finitely generated projective module. Then
M
is of finite presentation and hence
(a)
holds for
M
. Pulling back
M
, considered as a vector bundle over
\Re(A,z)
, to
\Re(A,z)^{+}
and
\Re(A,z)^{-}
, it follows from Remark
5.6

(1)
and the description of vector bundles on
[\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{1}]
in Proposition
2.4
that
M
satisfies
(b)
. Moreover,
M
is a direct summand of a finite graded free
B
-module (Proposition
1.6
). Since each graded piece of
B
is equal to
A
, it follows that
M_{j}
is a finite projective
A
-module for all
j
. Finally, since
M
is flat, the multiplication with
z
on
M
is injective. Since
z=tu=ut
, this implies that
t
and
u
are injective.

Let us show
(5)
. In this case
B=A[t,u]/(tu)
is the fiber product of graded rings
A[u]\times_{A}A[t]
, and therefore a quasi-coherent
\Re(A,0)
-module is a vector bundle if and only if its pullback to
\Re(A,0)^{+}
and
\Re(A,0)^{-}
is a vector bundle
[
Fer03
, 2.2]
which is equivalent to
(b)
, again by Remark
5.6

(1)
.

We now show
(3)
, so we assume that
z
is regular. We have already seen in
(2)
, that
(i)
implies
(ii)
. Clearly,
(ii)
implies
(iii)
,
(iv)
, and
(v)
.

Let us show that
(iii)
implies
(i)
. We apply Proposition
B.1
to
I=(t)\subseteq\Rees(A)
. Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be the quasi-coherent module on
\Re(A,z)
corresponding to
M
such that
\tilde{M}
is the pullback of
{\mathscr{E}}
to
\Spec B
. By
(1)
we know that
M
is a
B
-module of finite presentation. The restriction of
\tilde{M}
to
U=\Spec B\setminus V(t)
is the pullback of
{\mathscr{E}}{}_{|}{}_{\Re(A,z)^{\neq+}}
which corresponds to the
A
-module
M_{-\infty}
which by hypothesis is flat. The (non-dervied) restriction of
M
to
V(t)
is the pullback of the restriction of
{\mathscr{E}}
to
\Re(A,z)^{+}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}]
which is a vector bundle by Proposition
2.4
. It remain to show that
\Tor^{B}_{1}(B/tB,M)=0
. Since
z=ut
,
t\in B
is a regular element, i.e. one has
B/tB\cong(B\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}B)
in
D(B)
and hence
\Tor^{B}_{1}(B/tB,M)=\Ker(M\mathrel{\smash{\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}}}M)=0
.

To see that
(iv)
implies
(i)
one argues in the same manner applying Proposition
B.1
to
(u)\subseteq\Rees(A)
. Moreover, the same argument, now by applying Proposition
B.1
to
(z)\subseteq B
also shows that
(v)
implies
(i)
. Here we use that the flatness of
M/zM
is equivalent to Condition
(b)
, which we have already proved, and that condition
(c)
implies that
z\colon M_{j}\to M_{j}
is injective for all
j
and hence
\Tor_{1}^{B}(B/zB,M)=\Ker(z\colon M\to M)=0
.

It remains to show
(4)
. Again we apply Proposition
B.1
to
I=(z)\subseteq B
. The open subset
U=\Spec A[1/z]
does not contain any closed point because
z
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
A
, so the condition that
{\mathscr{E}}{}_{|}{}_{U}
is flat is unnecessary by Remark
B.2
. The module
M
corresponds to a module of finite presentation by
(a)
and its restriction to the vanishing locus of
z
is flat by
(b)
as we have seen in
(5)
. It remains to see that

\Tor_{1}^{B}(B/zB,M)=\Tor_{1}^{A}(A/z,M)=0,

where the equality holds by Lemma
5.9
below since
B
is a flat
A
-algebra (
[
GW20
, 14.22]
). But as an
A
-module,
M
is by
(c)
the direct sum of the finite projective
A
-modules
M_{j}
which implies (*).
∎

In the proof above we used to following easy fact.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
A\to B
and
B\to C
maps of rings and suppose that
A\to B
or
A\to C
is flat
2
2

2

or, more generally, that
A\to B
and
A\to C
are tor-independent
. Let
M
be a
B
-module. Then
\Tor^{B}_{n}(C\otimes_{A}B,M)=\Tor^{A}_{n}(C,M)
for all
n
.

Proof.

We have
(C\otimes_{A}B)\mathrel{\smash{\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}}}_{B}M=C\mathrel{\smash{\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}}}_{A}B\mathrel{\smash{\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}}}_{B}M=C\mathrel{\smash{\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}}}_{A}M
since
A\to B
and
A\to C
are tor-independent.
∎

5.2.
Vector bundles and
G
-bundles on
\Re(A,v)

We keep the notation from Section
5.1
and we set
R:=A/v(L)
as before. The closed immersion
B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}=\Re(A,v)^{0}\longrightarrow\Re(A,v)
yields a map of groupoids

(5.2.1)

\Bun_{G}(\Re(A,v))\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}).

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
G
be a smooth and affine group scheme over
A
and suppose that
(A,v(L))
is a henselian pair. Then (
5.2.1
) is full and essentially surjective and in particular we obtain an isomorphism

H^{1}(G,\Re(A,v))\overset{\sim}{\to}H^{1}(G,B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}).

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
Wed24
, 2.9]
.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

If
(A,v(L))
is a henselian pair, then every vector bundle
E
over
