R=A/v(L)
, lifts to a vector bundle
\tilde{E}
over
A
, and
\tilde{E}
is unique up to isomorphism. Moreover, every vector bundle over
A
is of this form. Hence every vector bundle
M=\bigoplus_{j}M_{j}
over
\Re(A,v)
is isomorphic to a vector bundle of the form

(5.2.2)

\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{E}_{j}\otimes_{A}B(j),

where
(E_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a family of finite projective
R
-modules which are almost all zero. Moreover the
E_{j}
are uniquely determined up to isomorphism.

In particular, we see that Zariski locally on
\Spec R
(not only on
\Spec A
),
M
is a finite graded free
B
-module.

Proposition 5.12
.

Suppose that
v\colon L\to A
is injective, set
I:=v(L)
, which is an invertible ideal of
A
, and let
\Re(A,I)
be the corresponding Rees stack.

(1)

The restriction functors

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle\textup{Vec}(\Re(A,I))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\textup{Vec}(\Re(A,I)^{\neq 0}),

\displaystyle\Bun_{G}(\Re(A,I))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}(\Re(A,I)^{\neq 0})

are fully faithful for every affine flat group scheme
G
over
A
, .

(2)

Suppose that
A
is a Dedekind domain and that
I={\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\cdots{\mathfrak{m}}_{r}
for
r\geq 1
pairwise different maximal ideals
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
. Then the restrictions
5.2.3
are equivalences for every reductive group scheme
G
over
A
.

Proof.

Let
B
be the underlying
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
A
-algebra of the filtered ring, set
X=\Spec B
and let
U:=X\setminus X^{0}
the complement of the fixed point locus. To show (1), by Theorem
3.4

(1)
we have to show that
{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\to j_{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{U}
is an isomorphism. This can be checked Zariski locally on
A
and hence we can assume that
I=(z)
for a regular element
z\in A
. Since
X
is affine, it suffices to show that restriction yields an isomorphism
\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})\overset{\sim}{\to}\Gamma(U,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})
.

By Section
5.1
we have
B\cong A[t,u]/(tu-z)
and
U
is the open subscheme
D(t)\cup D(u)
of
X=\Spec A[t,u]/(tu-z)
. Let
j\colon U\to X
be the inclusion.

Using the open affine covering
U=D(t)\cup D(u)
we see that
\Gamma(U,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})=A[t,t^{-1}]\cap A[u,u^{-1}]
where the intersection takes place in
A[1/z][t,t^{-1}]
which contains
A[u,u^{-1}]
via
u\mapsto zt^{-1}
(since
z
is regular,
A\to A[1/z]
is injective). But this intersection is equal to
A[t,u]/(tu-z)
, embedded into
A[1/z][t,t^{-1}]
by
t\mapsto t
and
u\mapsto zt^{-1}
.

To show Assertion (2) we will use Theorem
3.4

(2)
. To show that
B
is a regular ring of dimension
2
we may again work locally on
A
and hence we may assume that
B=A[t,u]/(tu-p_{1}\cdots p_{r})
for pairwise coprime prime elements
p_{i}\in A
. Then
(t,u)
is a regular system in
B
such that
B/(t,u)=A/(p_{1}\cdots p_{r})
is a product of fields. This shows that
B
is regular of dimension
2
and that
D(t)\cup D(u)
contains every point of codimension
\leq 1
, allowing us to apply Theorem
3.4

(2)
.
∎

Let us describe the essential image of (
5.2.3
) for arbitrary rings. We start with vector bundles. Recall that a vector bundle (resp a quasi-coherent module) over
\Re(A,I)^{\neq 0}
is given by a tripel
(E,E^{\prime},\Phi)
, where
E
and
E^{\prime}
are vector bundles (resp. modules) over
A
and where
\Phi\colon E[1/I]\overset{\sim}{\to}E^{\prime}[1/I]
is an isomorphism over
A[1/I]
. Then

(5.2.4)

\displaystyle\Gamma(\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0},{\mathscr{E}})

\displaystyle=\{\,(e,e^{\prime})\in E\times E^{\prime}\ ;\ \Phi(e)=e^{\prime}\,\}

\displaystyle=\{\,e\in E\ ;\ \Phi(e)\in E^{\prime}\,\}=\Phi^{-1}(E^{\prime})\cap E.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
\iota\colon\Re(A,I)^{\neq 0}\to\Re(A,I)
be the inclusion, which is a quasi-compact open immersion. Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be a quasi-coherent module over
\Re(A,I)^{\neq 0}
given by a triple
(E,E^{\prime},\Phi)
as above. Using the notation introduced in Remark
5.5
, the quasi-coherent module
\iota_{*}{\mathscr{E}}
over
\Re(A,I)
is given

M_{j}=I^{j}\Phi^{-1}(E^{\prime})\cap E,\qquad\text{intersection in $E[1/I]$},

t\colon M_{j}\to M_{j-1}
the inclusion,
u\colon I\otimes AM_{j}\to M_{j+1}
the multiplication.

Proof.

Set
{\mathcal{X}}:=\Re(A,v)
. Since we know that
\iota^{*}
is fully faithful, we have
\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\cong\id
. By (
1.3.1
) we have

\displaystyle M_{j}

\displaystyle=\Gamma({\mathcal{X}},\iota_{*}({\mathscr{E}})(j))

\displaystyle=\Gamma({\mathcal{X}},\iota_{*}({\mathscr{E}}\otimes\iota^{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(j))

\displaystyle=\Gamma(\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0},{\mathscr{E}}\otimes\iota^{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(j)),

where the second identity holds by the projection formula. By Example
5.7
,
{\mathscr{E}}\otimes\iota^{*}{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(j)
corresponds to the triple
(E,E^{\prime}\otimes I^{j},\Phi\otimes v^{-j})
and by (
5.2.4
) we therefore find

M_{j}=I^{j}\Phi^{-1}(E^{\prime})\cap E.

For this note that if we identify
L
with
I
, then
v
is just the inclusion and hence
v^{-j}\colon I^{\otimes-j}[1/I]\cong A[1/I]\to A[1/I]
is just the identity.

The map
t\colon M_{j}=\Gamma({\mathcal{X}},\iota_{*}({\mathscr{E}})(j))\to M_{j-1}
is induced by the inclusion
{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(j)\to{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(j-1)
, given by
\Fil^{d}\to\Fil^{d-1}
for
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, which shows that
t
is indeed just the inclusion. Then
u
has also the stated form since it is determined by
t
.
∎

Proposition 5.14
.

Suppose that
v\colon L\to A
is injective and set
I:=v(L)
. For a vector bundle on
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
given by
(E,E^{\prime},\Phi)
the following assertions are equivalent.

(i)

The vector bundle
(E,E^{\prime},\Phi)
is in the essential image of (
5.2.3
).

(ii)

The following conditions are satisfied.

(a)

M_{j}:=I^{j}\Phi^{-1}(E^{\prime})\cap E
(intersection in
E[1/I]
) is a finite projective
A
-module.

(b)

M_{j}=M_{j-1}
for
j\ll 0
and
M_{j}=IM_{j+1}
for
j\gg 0
.

(c)

M_{j}/(M_{j+1}+IM_{j-1})
is a finite projective
R
-module for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(iii)

The vector bundle
(E,E^{\prime},\Phi)
is Zariski locally on
\Spec A
isomorphic to a triple of the form
(A^{n},A^{n},\Phi)
, where
\Phi
is given by a diagonal matrix
\textup{diag}(z^{e_{1}},\dots,z^{e_{n}})
for a generator
z
of
I
, which exists Zariski locally, and
(e_{1},\dots,e_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{n}
, i.e.
e_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
e_{1}\geq\dots\geq e_{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\iota
be as in Lemma
5.13
. By
[
GW23
, 24.67]
, which easily generalizes to stacks, a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
is in the essential image of (
5.2.3
) if and only if
\iota_{*}({\mathscr{E}})
is a vector bundle. In Lemma
5.13
we have seen a description of
\iota_{*}({\mathscr{E}})
. To see that the conditions in
(ii)
just mean that
\iota_{*}({\mathscr{E}})
is a vector bundle we apply Proposition
B.1
with
I=(u,t)
. Conditions
(a)
and
(b)
imply that
\iota_{*}{\mathscr{E}}
is of finite presentation. As the
M_{j}
are finite projective, the maps
t
and
u
are injective which implies as in the proof of Proposition
5.8
that
\Tor^{B}_{1}(B/IB,M)=0
, since
(t,u)
is a regular system. By hypothesis the restriction of
\iota_{*}{\mathscr{E}}
to the non-vanishing locus
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
of
I
is locally free. Hence Proposition
B.1
shows that
\iota_{*}{\mathscr{E}}
is a vector bundle. Hence
(i)
and
(ii)
are equivalent.

By Proposition
1.6
, every vector bundle on
{\mathcal{X}}:=\Re(A,v)
is Zariski locally isomorphic to a direct sum of twisted line bundles
{\mathscr{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(e)
. By Example
5.7
we see therefore that
(i)
and
(iii)
are equivalent.
∎

Remark 5.15
.

The vector bundle
(A^{n},A^{n},\Phi)
in
(iii)
of Proposition
5.14
has the following more conceptual description which also generalizes to
G
-bundles for more general smooth affine group schemes
G
: Let
P_{0}
be the trivial
\GL_{n}
-bundle on
\Spec\Rees(A,v)
, let
(e_{1},\dots,e_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{+}
, and let
\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to GL_{n}
by the corresponding cocharacter
t\mapsto\textup{diag}(t^{e_{1}},\dots,t^{e_{n}})
. Then
\mu
defines a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on
P_{0}
and hence a
\GL_{n}
-bundle on
\Re(A,v)
. In other words, it is the trivial
\mu\backslash\GL_{n}
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{\mu}
on
\Re(A,v)
, see Definition
3.8
. It corresponds to the vector bundle
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}{\mathscr{O}}_{\Re(A,v)}(e_{i})
whose restriction is the vector bundle
(A^{n},A^{n},\Phi)
in
(iii)
of Proposition
5.14
.

As
\iota^{*}
is monoidal and exact, the full subcategory of vector bundles satisfying the equivalent conditions of Proposition
5.14
form an exact symmetric monoidal subcategory of the symmetric monoidal category of all vector bundles on
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
. We denote this subcategory by
\textup{Vec}(\Re(a,v)^{\neq 0})^{0}
.

Now assume that
A
is an
O
-algebra, where
O
is a Dedekind domain, and let
G
be an affine smooth group scheme over
O
.

Corollary 5.16
.

For a
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}
the following assertions are equivalent.

(i)

There exists a
G
-bundle
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Re(A,v)
such that
{\mathscr{E}}\cong\iota^{*}\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
.

(ii)

There exists an étale surjective map
\Spec O^{\prime}\to\Spec O
and a conjugacy class of cocharacters
[\mu]
of
G
defined over
O^{\prime}
such that
{\mathscr{E}}
is étale locally isomorphic to the
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
.

(iii)

The exact monoidal functor
\Rep(G)\to\textup{Vec}(\Re(a,v)^{\neq 0})
corresponding to
{\mathscr{E}}
factors through
\textup{Vec}(\Re(a,v)^{\neq 0})^{0}
.

5.3.
Colimits of truncations of
\Re(A,v)

In this section let
A
be a ring,
L
a line bundle, and
v\colon L\to A
be an
A
-linear map. We obtain a filtered ring
(A,(L^{\otimes j})_{j\geq 0})
as in Section
5.1
and the attached Rees stack
\Re(A,v)
. We fix also an ideal
J\subseteq A
such that
A
is
J
-adically complete.

For an integer
N\geq 1
let
\pi_{N}\colon A\to A/J^{N}
be the canonical map. Let
\pi_{N}^{*}(A,(L^{\otimes j}))=(A/J^{N},(L^{\otimes j}\otimes_{A}A/J^{N})_{j})
be its base change (Remark
2.14
). We obtain the attached graded Rees stack

\Re(A/J^{N},v)=\Re(A,v)\otimes_{A}A/J^{N}.

We also set
A/J^{\infty}:=A
.
For
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
, the canonical maps
A/J^{N^{\prime}}\to A/J^{N}
yield a closed immersion of algebraic stacks

(5.3.1)

\Re(A/J^{N},v)\to\Re(A/J^{N^{\prime}},v).

By Proposition
1.9
we have the following result.

Proposition 5.17
.

The natural map of stacks
\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N<\infty}\Re(A/J^{N},v)\longrightarrow\Re(A,v)
, where we form the colimit in the 2-category of Adams stacks, is an isomorphism.

To apply these results to
G
-bundles on
\Re(A,v)
, we now assume that
A
is an
O
-algebra for some noetherian regular ring
O
of dimension
\leq 1
and that
G
is a smooth affine group scheme over
O
. For
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
the closed immersion
\Re(A/J^{N},v)\to\Re(A/J^{N^{\prime}},v)
yields by pullback a map of groupoids

(5.3.2)

\Bun_{G}(\Re(A/J^{N^{\prime}},v))\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}(\Re(A/J^{N},v)).

Theorem 5.18
.

(1)

The maps (
5.3.2
) are full and essentially surjective for all
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
. In particular they induce isomorphisms

H^{1}(G,\Re(A/J^{N^{\prime}},v))\overset{\sim}{\to}H^{1}(G,\Re(A/J^{N},v)).

(2)

The maps (
5.3.2
) yield an equivalence

\Bun_{G}(\Re(A,v))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\lim_{N<\infty}\Bun_{G}(\Re(A/J^{N},v)).

Proof.

The fact that (
5.2.1
) is full and essentially surjective is a special case of Theorem
3.2
. Since
A
is
J
-adically complete,
(A,J)
is a henselian pair and therefore
\Bun_{G}(\Re(A_{N^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}(\Re(A_{N}))
is full and essentially surjective by
[
Wed24
, 2.6]
. This shows
(1)
.

Assertion
(2)
follows from Corollary
1.10
.
∎

5.4.
The frame of
(A,v,\phi)

We keep the notation
A
,
v\colon L\to A
, as before. Let
\phi\colon A\to A
be a ring endomorphism. Then
\phi
induces a map

(5.4.1)

\sigma\colon\Spec A\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\phi}{\longrightarrow}}}\Spec A\cong\Re(A,v)^{\neq 0}\hookrightarrow\Re(A,v)

and we obtain a frame attached to
(A,v,\phi)
and hence the frame stack
{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)
and its open substack
{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)^{\neq 0}
(Definition
4.6
).

Corollary 5.19
.

Let
(A,v,\phi)
be as above. Suppose that
v
is injective. Let
A
be an
O
-algebra, where
O
is a Dedekind domain, and let
G
be a smooth affine group scheme over
O
.

(1)

Pullback via
{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)^{\neq 0}\to{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)
yield fully faithful functors

(5.4.2)

\displaystyle\textup{Vec}{{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\textup{Vec}{{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)^{\neq 0}}

\displaystyle\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)^{\neq 0}).

(2)

A
G
-bundle on
{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi)^{\neq 0}
is in the essential image of (
5.4.2
) if and only if it is étale locally isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{\mu}
.

(3)

Suppose that
A
is a Dedekind domain,
v(L)
a product of pairwise different maximal ideals, and
G
is reductive. Then the functors (
5.4.2
) are equivalences of categories.

Proof.

This follows by combining Proposition
5.12
, Corollary
5.16
, and Lemma
4.9
.
∎

Now fix as in Section
5.3
a
\phi
-invariant ideal
J\subseteq A
such that
A
is
J
-adically complete. Then we obtain for all
N\geq 1
an induced frame on
A/J^{N}
and its frame stack
{\mathcal{F}}(A/J^{N},v,\phi)
. Since colimits commute with coequalizers we obtain from Proposition
5.17
, Example
1.8
and Theorem
5.18
the following result, where
G
is as in Section
5.3
.

Corollary 5.20
.

We have a natural isomorphism of Adams stacks

\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N<\infty}{\mathcal{F}}(A/J^{N},v,\phi)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi).

In particular, one has a natural equivalence

\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A,v,\phi))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\lim_{N<\infty}\Bun_{G}({\mathcal{F}}(A/J^{N},v,\phi))

for every smooth affine group scheme
G
over
O
.

5.5.
The case
v=0

If
v=0
, then
R=A
and

B=\Rees(A,0)=\Sym_{A}(L)\times_{A}A[t]=\{\,(f,g)\in\Sym_{A}(L)\times A[t]\ ;\ f_{0}=g(0)\,\}.

Hence
\Re(A,0)
is the algebraic stack glued together from
\Re(A,0)^{-}=A^{\Fil}
and
\Re(A,0)^{+}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec\Sym_{A}(L)]
along the closed substack
\Re(A,0)^{0}=B\mathbb{G}_{m,A}
.

Therefore a vector bundle over
\Re(A,0)
is a triple
(M,N,\alpha)
consisting of a vector bundle
M
on
A
with a decreasing filtration (Proposition
2.4
), a vector bundle
N
with an increasing
L
-filtration (see Remark
2.5
), and an isomorphism
\alpha\colon\gr(M)\overset{\sim}{\to}\gr(N)
.

Now suppose that we are given a ring endomorphism
\phi\colon A\to A
yielding a frame structure and an attached frame stack
{\mathcal{F}}(A,0,\phi)
as in Section
5.4
. In this case we obtain
{\mathcal{F}}(A,0,\phi)
by gluing the open substacks

\displaystyle\Re(A,0)^{-}\setminus\Re(A,0)^{0}

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec A[t,t^{-1}]]\cong\Spec A,

\displaystyle\Re(A,0)^{+}\setminus\Re(A,0)^{0}

\displaystyle=[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec\Sym_{A}(L)]\cong\Spec A

of
\Re(A,0)
via
\phi
.

Therefore a vector bundle on
{\mathcal{F}}(A,0,\phi)
is given by a vector bundle
N
with an increasing
L
-filtration
D
, a decreasing vector bundle filtration
C
on
\phi^{*}N
and an isomorphism
\alpha\colon\gr_{C}(\phi^{*}N)\overset{\sim}{\to}\gr^{D}(N)
.

We can also consider the following variant of
{\mathcal{F}}(A,0,\phi)
. Let
A^{\textup{$\phi$-BiZip}}
the stack defined by the pushout square

\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{A^{\Fil}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec\Sym_{A}(\phi^{*}L)]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\textup{$\phi$-BiZip}},}

where the left vertical map is the composition of the map
B\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,A}
induced by
\phi
and the zero section
B\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\to[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec\Sym_{A}(\phi^{*}L)]
. In
A^{\textup{$\phi$-BiZip}}
there are two open substacks
\Spec A\subseteq A^{\Fil}
and
\Spec A\subseteq[\mathbb{G}_{m,A}\backslash\Spec\Sym_{A}(\phi^{*}L)]
and we form the coequalizer of these two open immersions to obtain an Adams stack
A^{\textup{$\phi$-Zip}}
.

Then the canonical
\phi
-linear map
L\to\phi^{*}L
induces a map
A^{\textup{$\phi$-BiZip}}\to\Re(A,0)
and then by taking coequalizers a map

(5.5.1)

A^{\textup{$\phi$-Zip}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}(A,0,\phi).

A vector bundle on
A^{\textup{$\phi$-Zip}}
is given by a vector bundle
N
endowed with an increasing
L
-filtration
D
and a decreasing vector bundle filtration
C
and an isomorphism
\alpha\colon\phi^{*}\gr_{C}(N)\overset{\sim}{\to}\gr(N)
. Pullback under (
5.5.1
) sends
(N,C,D,\alpha)
to
(N,\phi^{*}C,D,\alpha)
.

6.
Moduli of truncated equi-characteristic shtukas

In this section, we apply the above general result to study moduli spaces of truncated local shtukas. We fix the following notation. Let
\kappa
be a field. From Section
6.3
on,
\kappa
will be a finite field. We also fix a reductive group scheme
G
over
O:=\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
. If
R
is any
\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
-algebra we denote by
G_{R}
its base change to
R
. Note that
G
is obtained via base change from a unique (up to isomorphism) reductive group over
\kappa
3
3

3

Let
G^{\prime}=G_{\kappa}\otimes_{\kappa}\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
. Then
G
and
G^{\prime}
have the same geometric based root datum therefore
G^{\prime}
is a form of
G
for the étale topology. These forms of
G
are classified by
H^{1}(\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]},\underline{\Aut}(G))
, where
\underline{\Aut}(G)
is the group scheme of automorphisms of
G
. Since
\underline{\Aut}(G)
is ind-quasi-affine and smooth over
\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
, we know that
H^{1}(\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]},\underline{\Aut}(G))\to H^{1}(\kappa,\underline{\Aut}(G))
is injective by
[
Bv22
, 2.1.7]
which shows
G^{\prime}\cong G
.
which we also call
G
.

Let
N
denote an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\cup\{\infty\}
, which we endow with its usual total order. For every
\kappa
-algebra
R
we set

R_{N}=R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}/(z^{N})\quad\text{for $N<\infty$},\qquad R_{\infty}:=R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}.

Hence
R_{0}
is the zero ring. For all
1\leq N\leq\infty
we endow
R_{N}
with the
z
-adic filtration (Example
5.1

(2)
).

Define the positive loop group, resp. the loop group as usually by

(6.0.1)

L^{+}G(R)=G(R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}),\qquad\qquad LG(R)=G(R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}).

We have for their truncations

L^{(N)}G(R):=G(R_{N}),

i.e.,
L^{(\infty)}G=L^{+}G
and
L^{(N)}(G)=\Res_{\kappa_{N}/\kappa}(G)
for
N<\infty
. Then

L^{+}G=\lim_{N<\infty}L^{(N)}(G).

We also set

L_{N}(G)=\Ker(L^{+}G\to L^{(N)}(G)),

i.e.,
L_{N}G(R)=\{\,g\in G(R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]})\ ;\ g\mapsto 1\in G(R_{N})\,\}
. In particular
L_{0}G:=L^{+}G
. Hence we find
L^{+}G/L_{N}(G)=L^{(N)}(G)
.

6.1.
The
N
-truncated Hecke stack

Let
R
be a
\kappa
-algebra. We now apply the results of Section
5
to
v\colon R_{N}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{z}{\longrightarrow}}}R_{N}
.

Definition 6.1
.

For
1\leq N\leq\infty
we call

R^{\Hck,N}:=\Re(R_{N},z)=[\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash\Spec(R_{N}[t,u]/(tu-z))]

the
N
-truncated Hecke stack
over
R
. For
N=\infty
we also set

R^{\Hck}:=\Re(R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]},z)^{\neq 0}\subseteq R^{\Hck,\infty}

and call it the
Hecke scheme
over
R
.

Remark 6.2
.

By Remark
5.2
, the scheme
R^{\Hck}
is obtained by gluing two copies of
\Spec R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
along
\Spec R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}
. Therefore the groupoid
\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck})
is equivalent to the groupoid of triples
({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2},\alpha)
, where
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
are
G
-bundles on
R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
and where
\Phi\colon{\mathscr{E}}_{2}[1/z]\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[1/z]
is an isomorphism of
G
-bundles over
R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}
. One has a similar description for the exact category of vector bundles on
R^{\Hck}
. This explains the terminology.

By Remark
5.3
,
R^{\Hck}
is semi-separated (but not separated) and it has an ample pair of line bundles. In particular, it satisfies the resolution property.

Proposition
5.12
and Corollary
5.16
yield the following result which will be essential for us in the sequel.

Proposition 6.3
.

Restriction yields fully faithful functors

(6.1.1)

\displaystyle\textup{Vec}(R^{\Hck,\infty})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\textup{Vec}(R^{\Hck}),

\displaystyle\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck,\infty})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck})

which is an equivalence if
R
is a field. The essential image for
G
-bundles of (
6.1.1
) are those
G
-bundles
{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck})
for which there exists a finite separable extension
\kappa^{\prime}
of
\kappa
and a conjugacy class of cocharacter
[\mu]
of
G
defined over
\kappa^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
such that
{\mathscr{E}}
is étale locally on
R
isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
(Definition
3.8
).

See also Proposition
5.14
for a description of the essential image of (
6.1.1
) for vector bundles.

By Theorem
5.18
we have

(6.1.2)

\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck,\infty})=\lim_{N<\infty}\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck,N})

If
R\to R^{\prime}
is a map of
\kappa
-algebras, it induces a map
R^{\prime\Hck,N}\to R^{\Hck,N}
for all
1\leq N\leq\infty
and we obtain a prestack over
\kappa

(6.1.3)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck,N)\colon\textup{$\kappa$-$\mathcal{A}$lg}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}\textup{rpd},\qquad R\mapsto\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck,N}).

Remark 6.4
.

For
N<\infty
we have
R_{N}\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}=R^{\prime}_{N}
and hence
R^{\Hck,N}\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}=R^{\prime\Hck,N}
. Therefore we find

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck,N)=\underline{\Hom}(\kappa^{\Hck,N},BG)

in this case.

Similarly, we obtain the
(local)
G
-Hecke stack

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)\colon\textup{$\kappa$-$\mathcal{A}$lg}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}\textup{rpd},\qquad R\mapsto\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck}).

In fact, it is well known is a stack for the fpqc topology. This will also follow from Proposition
6.5
, where we will see that
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck,N)
is a stack for the fpqc topology for all
N
.

The functors (
6.1.1
) are functorial in
R
and define a map of stacks

(6.1.4)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,\infty})\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck).

which is fully faithful on
R
-valued points. It is an equivalence of categories on
k
-valued points for every field extension
k
of
\kappa
.

Proposition 6.5
.

For
N<\infty
, the prestacks
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})
are algebraic stacks with affine diagonal. Moreover,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})
are stacks for the fpqc topology for all
N\leq\infty
.

Proof.

Let
N<\infty
. Recall that in this case
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})=\underline{\Hom}(\kappa^{\Hck,N},BG)
. Now
\kappa^{\Hck,N}
is an algebraic stack that is cohomologically projective over
\kappa
by
[
HLP23
, 4.2.5]
and hence formally proper over
\kappa
by
[
HLP23
, 4.2.1]
. Therefore
[
HLP23
, 5.1.1]
implies that
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})
is an algebraic with affine diagonal. Since its diagonal is affine, it is a stack for the fpqc topology.

By (
6.1.2
) we have
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,\infty})=\lim_{N<\infty}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})
which implies that
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,\infty})
is also a stack for the fpqc topology.
∎

Below in (
6.2.5
), we will describe
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})
explicity as a disjoint sum of quotient stacks. This will give a different proof of Proposition
6.5
.

6.2.
G
-bundles of type
[\mu]
over the
R^{\Hck,N}

For
1\leq N\leq\infty
, denote by
i\colon(R^{\Hck,N})^{0}=B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\to R^{\Hck,N}
the closed embedding of the fixed point locus. Pullback with
i
defines a map of groupoids
\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck,N})\to\Bun_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R})
that is functorial in
R
. Hence we obtain a map of stacks over
\kappa

(6.2.1)

\type\colon\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa}).

For every
\kappa
-algebra
R
the induced map of groupoids

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})(R)\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa})(R)

is essentially surjective and full, in particular, it induces a bijection on isomorphism classes (Theorem
5.18
). We obtain for
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
a commutative diagram of fpqc stacks

(6.2.2)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Let
{\bar{\kappa}}
be an algebraic closure of
\kappa
, let
(X^{*},\Phi,X_{*},\Phi{}^{\vee},\Delta)
be the geometric based root datum of
G
, and let
W_{G}
be its Weyl group (Section
3.2
). Then the decomposition (
3.2.5
) of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa})
yields via taking inverse images under the type morphism a decomposition into open and closed substacks

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}({\Hck,N})_{\kappa^{\prime}}=\coprod_{[\mu]\in X_{*}/W_{G}}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}({\Hck,N})

over some finite separable extension
\kappa^{\prime}
of
\kappa
, for instance a splitting field of
G
. More precisely, each open and closed substack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}({\Hck,N})
is defined over the field of definition
\kappa_{[\mu]}\subseteq{\bar{\kappa}}
of the conjugacy class
[\mu]
.

Let us give a description of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}({\Hck,N})
as a moduli problem. For this recall the standard
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
of type
[\mu]
on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa_{[\mu]}}
defined in Definition
3.8
. By definition
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa_{[\mu]}})
consists of those
G
-bundles that are étale locally isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
.
Hence Theorem
5.18
implies the following result.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
R
be a
\kappa_{[\mu]}
-algebra. Then a
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
over
R^{\Hck,N}
defines an
R
-valued point of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}({\Hck,N})
if and only if it is étale locally on
\Spec R
isomorphic to the pullback of
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
to
R^{\Hck,N}
under the canonical map
R^{\Hck,N}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa_{[\mu]}}
.

Remark 6.7
.

We can also use the above formalism to give a moduli theoretic description of Schubert cells in the affine Grassmannian of
G
:

The monomorphism
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck,\infty)\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)
induces an isomorphism of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Hck,\infty)
with a locally closed substack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Hck)
whose
R
-valued points can be described as those
G
-bundles on
R^{\Hck}
that are étale locally isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
.

Then
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)
can also be described as the étale quotient stack of the loop group

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)=[L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G].

The affine Grassmannian of
G
is defined as the quotient

\Gr_{G}:=LG/L^{+}G.

It parametrizes
G
-bundles
{\mathscr{E}}
over
R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
together with a trivialization of
{\mathscr{E}}
over
R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}
. Equivalently, it parametrizes
G
-bundles over
R^{\Hck}
, described as triples
({\mathscr{E}}_{0},{\mathscr{E}},\alpha)
, where
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}
is the trivial
G
-bundle.

The projection
\Gr_{G}\to\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)
makes
\Gr_{G}
into an
L^{+}G
-torsor over
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)
and we denote the preimage of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Hck)
under this projection by
\Gr_{G}^{[\mu]}
. It parametrizes
G
-bundles over
R^{\Hck}
as above which are étale locally on
R
isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
. This gives a moduli theoretic description of the Schubert cell
\Gr_{G}^{[\mu]}
.

Remark 6.8
.

Let
\kappa^{\prime}
be a finite separable extension of
\kappa
such that the conjugacy class
[\mu]
can be represented by a cocharacter
\mu
of
G_{\kappa^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}}
. For a
\kappa^{\prime}
-algebra
R
, the groupoid
\Bun^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Hck)
can then also be described as the full subgroupoids of triples
({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2},\alpha)\in\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck})
in
\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck})
such that there exists a an étale
covering
\Spec R^{\prime}\to\Spec R
yielding
\pi\colon\Spec R^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}\to\Spec R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
such that
\pi^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and
\pi^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
are trivial
G
-bundles and that via some (equivalently, any) trivializations
\pi^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\cong G_{R^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}}\cong\pi^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
, the isomorphism
\alpha_{R^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}}
is given by
g\mapsto hg
for an element
h\in G({R^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}})\mu(z)G({R^{\prime}\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}})\subseteq G(R^{\prime}\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)})
.

We will now describe
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Hck,N)
more explicitly as quotient stack. We use the constructions of Section
3.3
. We define the display group, i.e., the group presheaf on the category of
\kappa_{[\mu]}
-algebras

(6.2.3)

E_{N}(G,[\mu])\colon R\mapsto\Hom_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}(R^{\Hck,N},[\mu]\backslash G_{\kappa_{[\mu]}})

see (
3.3.1
).

Remark 6.9
.

For
N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
, the closed immersions
R^{\Hck,N}\to R^{\Hck,N^{\prime}}
induces maps of group schemes

(6.2.4)

E_{N^{\prime}}(G,[\mu])\longrightarrow E_{N}(G,[\mu]).

Proposition 6.10
.

(1)

For
N<\infty
, the presheaf
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is representable by a smooth affine group scheme over
\kappa_{[\mu]}
.

(2)

One has
E_{\infty}(G,[\mu])=\lim_{N<\infty}E_{N}(G,[\mu])
, in particular
E_{\infty}(H,[\mu])
is representable by an pro-smooth affine group scheme over
\kappa_{[\mu]}
.

(3)

For all
N\leq\infty
one has

(6.2.5)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(R^{\Hck,N})\cong BE_{N}(G,[\mu])

Proof.

We use Proposition
3.24
. With the notation of loc. cit. one has
(B_{R})_{i}=R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}/(z^{N})
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. The functor
R\mapsto R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}/(z^{N})
is representable by a product of
N
-copies of
\mathbb{A}_{R}^{1}
. Therefore
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is representable by an affine group scheme for all
N
and it is of finite type for
N<\infty
by Proposition
3.24

(1)
. Since
R^{\Hck,\infty}=\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N<\infty}R^{\Hck,N}
by Proposition
5.17
, we have
E_{\infty}(G,[\mu])=\lim_{N<\infty}E_{N}(G,[\mu])
. For
N<\infty
,
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is smooth by Proposition
3.24

(2)
. Finally, (
6.2.5
) is a special case of Proposition
3.29
.
∎

Definition 6.11
.

The smooth affine group scheme
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is called the
N
-truncated
(G,[\mu])
-display group
.

Next we will describe the affine group schemes
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
more explicitly. To simplify notation, we assume from now on that
[\mu]
can be represented by a cocharacter
\mu
of
G
over the reflex field
\kappa_{[\mu]}
. This is always the case if
G
is quasi-split, for instance if
\kappa
is a finite field as will be the case starting with the next section anyway.

Proposition 6.12
.

(1)

For every
\kappa
-algebra
R
one has

(6.2.6)

E_{\infty}(G,\mu)(R)=\{\,g\in G(R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]})\ ;\ \mu(z)g\mu(z)^{-1}\in G(R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]})\subseteq G(R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)})\,\},

i.e.,
E_{\infty}(G,\mu)=L^{+}G\cap\mu^{-1}L^{+}G\mu\subseteq LG
.

(2)

For
1\leq N<\infty
one has

(6.2.7)

E_{N}(G,\mu)=E_{\infty}(G,\mu)/(L_{N}G\cap\mu^{-1}L_{N}G\mu).

Proof.

Let
E^{\prime}(G,\mu)
be the display group associated to
R\mapsto\Spec R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}
. By Example
3.25
we have
E^{\prime}(G,\mu)=LG
. The open immersions
\Spec R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}\to R^{\Hck}\to R^{\Hck,\infty}
define by Remark
3.26
a monomorphism
E_{\infty}(G,\mu)\to LG
. A straight forward calculation using the first equality of (
3.3.2
) shows that the image is precisely as described in (
6.2.6
). The second assertion follows then from Proposition
3.27
.
∎

There is also a different description of
E_{1}(G,\mu)
using the groups
P^{\pm}(\mu)
and
U^{\pm}(\mu)
from Remark
1.5
. To ease notation, we simply write
\Cent_{G}(\mu)
instead of
\Cent_{G_{\kappa_{[\mu]}}}(\mu)
.

Proposition 6.13
.

The 1-truncated
(G,\mu)
-display group
E_{1}(G,\mu)
is given by the smooth affine group scheme
\Cent_{G}(\mu)\rtimes(U^{-}(\mu)\times U^{+}(\mu))
. In particular,
E_{1}(G,\mu)
is a smooth connected algebraic group over
\kappa_{[\mu]}
of dimension
\dim G
.

This description can be deduced from (
6.2.7
), but we will give a direct proof here.

Proof.

The stack
R^{\Hck,1}
is obtained by glueing
[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash(\mathbb{A}_{R}^{1})^{-}]
and
[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash(\mathbb{A}_{R}^{1})^{+}]
along
B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}=[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash\{0\}]
(Section
5.5
). Therefore we find by Example
3.22

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{\mu}(1\text{-}\overline{\Hck})=BP^{-}(\mu)\times_{B\Cent_{G}(\mu)}BP^{+}(\mu)=BP^{-}(\mu)\times_{\Cent_{G}(\mu)}P^{+}(\mu),

where the last equality holds by Proposition
6.14
below. Since
P^{\pm}(\mu)=\Cent_{G}(\mu)\rtimes U^{\pm}(\mu)
, this implies the claim.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a site, let
{\mathcal{I}}
be a finite category, and let
i\mapsto G_{i}
be an
{\mathcal{I}}
-diagram of sheaves of groups in
{\mathcal{C}}
such that for every arrow
i\to j
the map
G_{i}\to G_{j}
is surjective. Set
G:=\lim_{i}G_{i}
. Then the canonical map of stacks in groupoids

\Psi\colon BG\longrightarrow\lim_{i}BG_{i}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By
[
Bie24
, 2.1]
,
\Psi
is fully faithful. Let
(E_{i})_{i\in I}
be in
\lim_{i}BG_{i}
. By loc. cit. it suffices to show that the
G
-pseudo-torsor
\lim_{i}E_{i}
has locally a section. But this follows easily from the hypothesis that all transition maps are epimorphisms: Let
U
be an object in
{\mathcal{C}}
. Since the number of objects in
{\mathcal{I}}
is finite, we find a covering
(U_{\alpha}\to U)_{\alpha}
such that
E_{i}(U_{\alpha})\neq\emptyset
for all
i
. Since the number of morphisms in
{\mathcal{I}}
is finite and since all transition maps are epimorphisms, we find, after passing to a refinement of
(U_{\alpha}\to U)
, even an element in
E(U_{\alpha})=\lim_{i}E_{i}(U_{\alpha})
for all
\alpha
.
∎

Let
{\mathscr{E}}
be any
G
-bundle of type
[\mu]
on
R^{\Hck,N}
. By Proposition
3.29
we see that
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is a strong inner form of
\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}})
. In particular
E_{N}(G,[\mu])\cong\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{E}})
if
R
is a separably closed field or a finite field.

Example 6.15
.

For
G=\GL_{n}
the calculations in
[
Lau21
, 3.4]
carry over verbatim: In this case
[\mu]
is given by a tuple
(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\mu_{1}\geq\mu_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{n}
. Then for every local
\kappa
-algebra
R
any
\GL_{n}
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
of type
[\mu]
on
R^{\Hck,N}
is of the form

{\mathscr{E}}=\bigoplus_{i}{\mathscr{O}}_{R^{\Hck,N}}(-\mu_{i})

and
\underline{\End}({\mathscr{E}})(R)
can be identified with the ring of matrices
A=(a_{ij})
with

a_{ij}\in\Hom_{R^{\Hck,N}}({\mathscr{O}}(-\mu_{j}),{\mathscr{O}}(-\mu_{i}))\cong\Rees(R_{N},z)_{\mu_{j}-\mu_{i}}\cong R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}/(z^{N}).

The entries with
\mu_{i}=\mu_{j}
form a sequence of square matrices
A_{1},\dots,A_{r}
along the diagonal, say of size
n_{1},\dots,n_{r}
with entries in
\Rees(R_{N},z)_{0}=R_{N}
and an endomorphism given by a matrix
A
is an automorphism if and only if
A_{i}\in M_{n_{i}}(R_{N})
is invertible (or, equivalently, the image of
A_{i}
in
M_{n_{i}}(R)
is invertible).

This shows in particular that
E_{N+1}(\GL_{n},[\mu])\to E_{N}(\GL_{n},[\mu]))
is surjective and that its kernel is a vector group of dimension
n^{2}=\dim(\GL_{n})
.

The last assertion holds in general.

Proposition 6.16
.

For all
1\leq N<\infty
, the truncation map
E_{N+1}(G,[\mu])\to E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is a surjective homomorphism of smooth algebraic groups. Its kernel is a vector group of dimension
\dim(G)
.

Proof.

The truncation map
E_{N+1}(G,[\mu])\to E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is surjective on
R
-valued points for every
\kappa
-algebra
R
by Proposition
3.27
. By (
6.2.7
), its kernel is given by

(L_{N}G\cap\mu^{-1}L_{N}G\mu)/(L_{N+1}G\cap\mu^{-1}L_{N+1}G\mu),

which is a vector group of dimension
\dim(G)
.
∎

Corollary 6.17
.

(1)

For all
1\leq N<\infty
, the display group
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is a smooth connected affine group scheme of dimension
N\dim(G)
. Its maximal reductive quotient is isomorphic to
\Cent_{G}(\mu)
and its unipotent radical is a successive extension of vector groups.

(2)

For all
1\leq N\leq\infty
, every
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
-bundles for the fpqc topology is already étale locally trivial.

Proof.

In Proposition
6.13
we saw the results for
N=1
and we conclude for
N<\infty
by Proposition
6.16
. The last assertion of
(1)
follows from Proposition
6.10
.

Assertion
(2)
holds for
N<\infty
since
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
is smooth. It holds then for
N=\infty
by
[
RS20
, A.4.8]
using
(1)
.
∎

6.3.
The truncated shtuka stack

From now on let
p
be a prime number, and let
\kappa=\mathbb{F}_{q}
be a finite field of characteristic
p
with
q
elements.

For every
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra
R
and for all
1\leq N\leq\infty
we define

\phi\colon R_{N}\to R_{N},\qquad\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}z^{i}\mapsto\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}^{q}z^{i}

and let

\sigma\colon\Spec R_{N}\to R^{\Hck,N}

be the induced map as in (
5.4.1
).

We obtain a frame
(R_{N},z,\sigma)
and morphisms

\displaystyle\tau\colon

\displaystyle\Spec R_{N}\overset{\sim}{\to}(R^{\Hck,N})^{\neq+}\hookrightarrow R^{\Hck,N},

\displaystyle\sigma\colon

\displaystyle\Spec R_{N}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\phi}{\longrightarrow}}}\Spec R_{N}\overset{\sim}{\to}(R^{\Hck,N})^{\neq-}\hookrightarrow R^{\Hck,N}.

Definition 6.18
.

Let
R
be an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra and
1\leq N\leq\infty
. The frame stack attached to the frame
(R_{N},z,\sigma)
is called the
N
-truncated shtuka stack over
R
. It is denoted by
R^{\Sht,N}
, i.e.,

R^{\Sht,N}=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.98914pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.98914pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Spec R_{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.74506pt\raise 6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.98914pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.27202pt\raise-6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.98914pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.98914pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R^{\Hck,N}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)

We also set

R^{\Sht}:=(R^{\Sht,\infty})^{\neq 0}=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.63086pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.63086pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Spec R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.87515pt\raise 6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.63086pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.40211pt\raise-6.65971pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.63086pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.63086pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R^{\Hck}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

and call it the
shtuka stack over
R
.

Remark and Definition 6.19
.

By definition a vector bundle over
R^{\Sht,N}
is the same as a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
over
R^{\Hck,N}
together with an isomorphism
\sigma^{*}{\mathscr{E}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\tau^{*}{\mathscr{E}}
over
\Spec R_{N}
. A similar description holds for
G
-bundles, i.e., one has

(6.3.1)

\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht,N})=\lim(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.04945pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-35.04945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Bun_{G}(R^{\Hck,N})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.66444pt\raise 7.82486pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.04945pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.1914pt\raise-7.82486pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.04945pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.04945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Bun_{G}(\Spec R_{N})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

This yields indeed a geometrization (in the sense of the introduction) for local shtukas:

Proposition 6.20
.

The category of vector bundles on
\Sht_{R}
is equivalent to the category of pairs
({\mathscr{E}},\Phi)
consisting of a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
and an isomorphism of vector bundles
\Phi\colon\phi^{*}({\mathscr{E}})[1/z]\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathscr{E}}[1/z]
over
R\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}
.

One has an analogous description for the groupoid of
G
-bundles on
\Sht_{R}
. In other words,
\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht})
is the groupoid of local
G
-shtukas over
R
.

Proof.

A vector bundle on
\Sht_{R}
is the same as a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
on
R^{\Hck}
together with an isomorphism
\alpha\colon\sigma^{*}{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}\tau^{*}{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
. By Remark
6.2
,
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
itself corresponds to a triple
({\mathscr{E}}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{2},\Phi)
, where
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
are vector bundles on
\Spec R\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
and
\Phi
is an isomorphism
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}[1/z]\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}[1/z]
. Then
\alpha
is an isomorphism
\phi^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and sending
({\mathscr{E}},\Phi)
to the tuple
(\phi^{*}{\mathscr{E}}_{2},{\mathscr{E}}_{2},\Phi,\id)
defines the desired equivalence.

The proof for
G
-bundles is the same.
∎

For
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
one has a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Spec R_{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{\Hck,N}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec R_{N^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{\Hck,N^{\prime}},}

where the horizontal maps are either the maps
\sigma
or the maps
\tau
and where the vertical maps are the canonical closed immersions. Hence one obtains a map

(6.3.2)

R^{\Sht,N}\longrightarrow R^{\Sht,N^{\prime}}.

As a special case of Corollary
5.19
and Corollary
5.20
we obtain the following result.

Theorem 6.21
.

Let
R
be a
\kappa
-algebra.

(1)

In the 2-category of Adams stacks the maps (
6.3.2
) induce an isomorphism

\mathop{\textup{colim}}_{N<\infty}R^{\Sht,N}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R^{\Sht,\infty}.

In particular we have

\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht,\infty})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\lim_{N<\infty}\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht,N})

(2)

Pullback via the inclusion
R^{\Sht}\hookrightarrow R^{\Sht,\infty}
yields a fully faithful functor

(6.3.3)

\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht,\infty})\longrightarrow\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht}).

Its essential image consists of those
G
-bundles that are étale locally on
R
isomorphic to the
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
attached to the conjugacy class of
\mu
.

The functor (
6.3.3
) is an equivalence if
R
is a field.

We can now define the moduli stack of truncated
G
-bundles as follows.

Definition 6.22
.

For all
1\leq N\leq\infty
and every
\kappa
-algebra
R
we define

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Sht,N)(R):=\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht,N}),\qquad\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Sht)(R):=\Bun_{G}(R^{\Sht}).

The prestack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Sht,N)
is an fpqc-stack by (
6.3.1
) and Proposition
6.5
. By Proposition
6.20
,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Hck)
is the moduli stack of local
G
-shtukas and hence also an fpqc-stack.

Again, we have a decomposition according to the type over some splitting extension
\kappa^{\prime}
of
\kappa

(6.3.4)

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Sht,N)_{\kappa^{\prime}}\cong\coprod_{[\mu]\in X_{*}/W}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)_{\kappa^{\prime}},

and each open and closed substack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)_{\kappa^{\prime}}
is defined over the reflex field
\kappa_{[\mu]}
of the conjugacy class
[\mu]
.

Let us describe the stack
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)
as a quotient stack. As in (
4.3.4
) we obtain for all
1\leq N\leq\infty
maps of affine group schemes over
\kappa

\tau_{N},\sigma_{N}\colon E_{N}(G,[\mu])\longrightarrow L^{(N)}G.

Using the description in Proposition
6.12
,
\tau_{\infty}
is given by the inclusion
L^{+}G\cap\mu^{-1}L^{+}G\mu\hookrightarrow L^{+}G
and
\sigma_{\infty}
is given by

\sigma_{\infty}(g)=\phi(\mu(z)g\mu(z)^{-1}).

For
1\leq N<\infty
, functoriality shows that
\tau_{N}
is induced by

L^{+}G\cap\mu^{-1}L^{+}G\mu\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\tau_{\infty}}{\longrightarrow}}}L^{+}G\longrightarrow L^{+}G/L_{N}G=L^{(N)}(G),

and
\sigma_{N}
is induced by

L^{+}G\cap\mu^{-1}L^{+}G\mu\mathrel{\smash{\overset{\sigma_{\infty}}{\longrightarrow}}}L^{+}G\longrightarrow L^{+}G/L_{N}G=L^{(N)}(G.

For
1\leq N\leq\infty
, we obtain an action of the affine flat group scheme
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
on the affine flat scheme
L^{(N)}(G)
by

(e,g)\mapsto\tau_{N}(e)g\sigma_{N}(e).

By our general results about
G
-bundles on frame stacks, we obtain now immediately the following description of the moduli space of
N
-truncated shtukas.

Theorem 6.23
.

For all
1\leq N\leq\infty
we have an equivalence of fpqc-stacks

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)\cong[E_{N}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{(N)}(G)].

For
N<\infty
,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)
is a smooth algebraic stack of relative dimension
0
over
\kappa_{[\mu]}
with geometric connected components.

Proof.

The description of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)
as a quotient stack is a special case of Theorem
4.12
. For
N<\infty
,
L^{(N)}(G)
is a smooth (geometrically) connected affine scheme of dimension
N\dim(G)
over
\kappa
. The same holds for the group schemes
E_{N}(G,[\mu])
by Corollary
6.17
. Therefore
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)
is a geometrically connected smooth algebraic stack of relative dimension
0
.
∎

Corollary 6.24
.

For all
1\leq N\leq\infty
and for every strictly henselian local
\kappa
-algebra
R
we have a functorial bijection

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Sht,N)(R)\cong[E_{N}(G,[\mu])(R)\backslash G(R_{N})].

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
6.23
and Proposition
6.17

(2)
.
∎

We next relate
R^{\Sht,1}
to the zip stack
R^{\Zip}
defined in
[
Yay24
]
, where Yaylali proved that the groupoid of
G
-bundles of
R^{\Zip}
is equivalent to the groupoid of
G
-zips over
R
, see
[
PWZ15
]
for the notion of
G
-zips. Let us recall the definition
R^{\Zip}
.

Definition and Remark 6.25
.

For every
\kappa
-algebra
R
let
\Frob_{R}
the
q
-Frobenius
a\mapsto a^{q}
on
R
. Define an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-scheme
Z_{R}
by the pushout diagram

\textstyle{\Spec R\sqcup\Spec R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\sqcup\infty}
\scriptstyle{\id_{R}\sqcup\Frob_{R}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{Z_{R}.}

By
[
Fer03
, 7.1]
the pushout exists in the category of schemes. If we let act
\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{q}}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
on
\Spec R
trivially and on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}
by the usual action

\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{q}}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}=\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\times_{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R},

then
\id_{R}\sqcup\Frob_{R}
and
0\sqcup\infty
are
\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-equivariant and we can form the quotient stack over
\mathbb{F}_{q}

(6.3.5)

R^{\Zip}:=[\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{q}}\backslash Z_{R}].

The map
i\colon\Spec R\to Z_{R}
is a closed immersion corresponding to an
R
-valued point
z_{0}\in Z_{R}(R)
.

We can describe
R^{\Zip}
also as follows. Let

(6.3.6)

D_{R}=\{\,(f,g)\in R[t]\times R[u]\ ;\ f(0)^{q}=g(0)\,\}.

This is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra with
\deg(t)=-1
and
\deg(u)=1
, as before. Let
\tau^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\colon\Spec R\to[\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{q}}\backslash\Spec D_{R}]
be the morphisms induced by the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded maps
(f,g)\mapsto f\in R[x,x^{-1}]
and
(f,g)\mapsto g\in R[x,x^{-1}]
. Then

(6.3.7)

R^{\Zip}=\mathop{\textup{colim}}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.19676pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.19676pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Spec R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.0004pt\raise 8.05057pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.19676pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.52736pt\raise-8.05057pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.19676pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.19676pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{[\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{q}}\backslash\Spec D_{R}]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces).

Proposition 6.26
.

There are a universal homeomorphisms

(6.3.8)

R^{\Sht,1}\longrightarrow R^{\Zip}\longrightarrow R^{\Sht,1}

of algebraic stacks over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, functorial in
R
.

Proof.

Set

C_{R}:=R[t,u]/(tu)=\{\,(f,g)\in R[t]\times R[u]\ ;\ f(0)=g(0)\,\}.

Then
R^{\Sht,1}=[\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash\Spec C_{R}]
and the maps (
6.3.8
) are induced by the map of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebras

\displaystyle C_{R}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow D_{R},\qquad(f,g)\mapsto(f^{(q)},g),

\displaystyle D_{R}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow C_{R},\qquad(f,g)\mapsto(f,g^{(q)}),

where
(\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}t^{i})^{(q)}=\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}^{q}t^{i}
, and similarly for
g^{(q)}
.
∎

Let
G{-}\textup{Zip}^{\mu}
be the stack of
G
-zips of type
\mu

[
PWZ15
]
. Since we have
G{-}\textup{Zip}^{\mu}(R)=\Bun^{[\mu]}_{G}(R^{\Zip})
by
[
Yay24
, A.5]
we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 6.27
.

Pullbacks via (
6.3.8
) yield universal homeomorphism of algebraic stacks

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,1)\longrightarrow G{-}\textup{Zip}^{\mu}\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,1).

Remark 6.28
.

One can view the above results as a reformulation of Theorem B and Theorem C from Qijun Yan
[
Yan25
]
.

6.4.
Traverso bounds for truncated
G
-shtukas

We keep the above notation.

Proposition 6.29
.

For all
\infty\geq N^{\prime}>N\geq 1
and for every
\kappa
-algebra
R
, the truncation map
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N^{\prime})(R)\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N)(R)
is locally for the étale topology on
R
essentially surjective.

It is easy to give examples that the truncation map is usually not surjective on morphisms.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.23
we have
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N)=[E_{N}(G,\mu)\backslash L^{(N)}(G)]
, hence any
R
-valued point of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N)
lifts étale locally on
R
to an
R
-valued point of
L^{(N)}(G)
because
E_{N}(G,\mu)
is a smooth group scheme by Corollary
6.17
. Since
G
is smooth and
(R_{N^{\prime}},(z^{N}))
is henselian, any
R
-valued point of
L^{(N)}G
lifts to an
R
-valued point of
L^{(N^{\prime})}G
.
∎

Remark 6.30
.

The proof of Proposition
6.29
shows that the obstruction to lift an
R
-valued point of
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N)
lies in
H^{1}(R,E_{N}(G,[\mu]))\cong H^{1}(R,\Cent_{G}(\mu))
, where the identity follows from Corollary
6.17

(1)
.

Theorem 6.31
.

There exists an
N_{0}
such the truncation map
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N^{\prime})\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N)
is bijective on isomorphism classes of
k
-valued points for every
\infty\geq N^{\prime}\geq N\geq N_{0}
and every algebraically closed extension
k
of
\kappa
.

Since
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Sht,\infty)(k)=\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Sht)(k)
by Theorem
6.21

(2)
we obtain in particular:

Corollary 6.32
.

There exists an
N_{0}
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
and every algebraically closed extension
k
of
\kappa
truncation yields a bijection between the set of isomorphism classes of local
G
shtukas of type
\mu
over
k
and the set of isomorphism classes of
N
-truncated local
G
-shtukas of type
\mu
over
k
.

We first recall some ingredients for the proof of Theorem
6.31
. We choose a Borel pair
T\subset B\subset G
of
G
and a conjugacy class
[\mu]\in X_{*}/W_{G}
. Let
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
be the dominant representative of
[\mu]
, defined over a finite extension of
\kappa
. Let
b\in LG(\bar{\kappa})
. We consider the associated affine Deligne-Lusztig variety, that is the locally closed reduced subscheme of the affine Grassmannian of
G
defined over
\bar{\kappa}
whose
\bar{\kappa}
-valued points are given by

X_{\mu}(b)(\bar{\kappa})=\{g\in LG(\bar{\kappa})/L^{+}G(\bar{\kappa})\mid g^{-1}b\phi(g)\in L^{+}G(\bar{\kappa})\mu(z)L^{+}G(\bar{\kappa})\}.

It is non-empty if and only if the
\phi
-conjugacy class
[b]
of
b
is contained in some finite subset
B(G,\mu)
of the Kottwitz set
B(G)
of
\phi
-conjugacy classes. The closed affine Deligne-Lusztig variety is defined as
X_{\leq\mu}(b)=\bigcup_{\mu^{\prime}\leq\mu}X_{\mu^{\prime}}(b)
. It is a closed subscheme of the affine Grassmannian. Let
J_{b}
be the automorphism group of
b
, which is a reductive group over
\kappa\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)}
with
J_{b}(\kappa\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)})
acting on
X_{\mu}(b)
and
X_{\leq\mu}(b)
for every
\mu
. We write
K=L^{+}G(\bar{\kappa})
.

Theorem 6.33

(
[
CN16
]
Prop. 8,
[
HV20
]
Lemma 5.3)
.

Let
b\in K\mu(z)K
. Then there is a finite subset
M_{0}(b,\mu)\subset X_{*}(T)_{\dom}
such that

X_{\mu}(b)\subseteq J_{b}(\kappa\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)})\cdot\coprod_{\xi\in M_{0}(b,\mu)}K\xi(z)K.

Taking the union over the finitely many classes
[b]\in B(G,\mu)
this implies the next corollary.

Corollary 6.34
.

For every
[b]\in B(G,\mu)
choose some representative
b\in[b]
. Then there is a finite subset
S\subset X_{*}(T)_{\dom}
such that for every
g\in K\mu(z)K
, there is an
h\in K\xi(z)K
for some
\xi\in S
such that
h^{-1}g\phi(h)=b
for one of the finitely many elements
b
chosen above.

Proof.

Let
S
be the union over all
[b]\in B(G,\mu)
of the sets
M_{0}(b,\mu_{b})
where
\mu_{b}
is such that
b\in K\mu_{b}(z)K
. Let
g
be as above, in particular
[g]\in B(G,\mu)
. Let
b
be the fixed representative of
[g]
. There is an
h\in LG(\bar{\kappa})
with
h^{-1}b\phi(h)=g
, in particular,
h\in X_{\mu}(b)
. We may replace
h
by another element of its
J_{b}(\kappa\mathopen{(\!(}z\mathclose{)\!)})
-orbit without changing these properties. Thus the theorem shows that we may choose
h\in K\xi(z)K
for some
\xi\in S
.
∎

Let
\widetilde{W}=N_{G}(T)(\bar{\kappa}(\!(z)\!))/T(\bar{\kappa}[\![z]\!])
be the extended affine Weyl group of
G
. An element
x\in\widetilde{W}
is called fundamental if the Iwahori double coset
IxI
(where
I
is the inverse image of
B
under the projection map
L^{+}G\rightarrow G
) is
I
-
\phi
-conjugate to
x
, compare
[
Nie15
]
for details and equivalent characterizations. By
[
Nie15
]
, Thm. 1.4, we may choose the representatives
b
in the corollary to be at the same time representatives of fundamental elements of
\widetilde{W}
.

Consider
I
as a parahoric group scheme over
O=\kappa\mathopen{[\![}z\mathclose{]\!]}
, and for every
n\in\mathbb N
let
I_{n}
denote the kernel
I(O)\rightarrow I(O/(z^{n}))
.

Lemma 6.35
.

Let
x
be a fundamental element in
\widetilde{W}
, let
M
be the centralizer of its Newton point and choose a representative in
LM(k)
of
x
, also denoted by
x
. Then for every
n\in\mathbb N
, the map
I_{n}\rightarrow I_{n}xI_{n}
with
i\mapsto ix\phi(i)
is surjective.

Proof.

For
n=0
, this is a well-known property of fundamental alcoves, see for example
[
GHN15
]
, Theorem 3.3.1. Assume that
n>0
. In particular, we then have
[I_{n},I_{n}]\subseteq I_{n+1}
. Using induction, it is enough to show that
I_{n}\cdot_{\phi}I_{n+1}xI_{n+1}\rightarrow I_{n}xI_{n}
is surjective, where
I_{n}
acts by
\phi
-conjugation.

Being fundamental and basic in
M
, the element
x\in LM(k)
is a representative of a length 0 element of the extended affine Weyl group of
M
(compare
[
Nie15
]
). Let
P
be the parabolic subgroup of
G
defined by the Newton point of
x
and let
N
be its unipotent radical. Let
\bar{N}
be the unipotent radical of the opposite parabolic. Let
I_{N,n},I_{\bar{N},n}
be the intersections with
I_{n}
. Since
I_{n}/I_{n+1}
is commutative, it is enough to show that for
I^{\prime}=I_{M,n},I_{N,n}
, or
I_{\bar{N},n}
, the map
I^{\prime}\cdot_{\phi}I_{n+1}xI_{n+1}\rightarrow I^{\prime}I_{n+1}xI_{n+1}I^{\prime}
with
i\mapsto ix\phi(i)
is surjective. For
I^{\prime}=I_{M,n}
recall that
x
is of length
0
in
M
and thus,
I_{M}=I\cap M
is stable under conjugation by
x
. Hence the same also holds for each
I_{M,n}=I_{n}\cap M
. Using the commutativity of
I_{n}/I_{n+1}
, it is enough to show that

I_{M,n}/I_{M,n+1}\rightarrow I_{M,n}xI_{M,n}/I_{M,n+1}=xI_{M,n}/I_{M,n+1}

with
g\mapsto g^{-1}x\phi(g)
is surjective. This in turn is a consequence of Lang’s theorem. Now consider
I^{\prime}=I_{N,n}
. Since
x
is fundamental, and
I
and
N
are
\phi
-stable, we have
x\phi(I_{N})x^{-1}\subseteq I_{N}
, and thus also
x\phi(I_{N,n})x^{-1}\subseteq I_{N,n}
for every
n
. Similarly,
I_{N,n}xI_{N,n}=I_{N,n}x
. Furthermore,
N
is the unipotent radical of the parabolic defined by the Newton point of
x
, or equivalently by any positive power of
x\phi
that is of the form
x^{\prime}\phi^{c}
where
x^{\prime}
is a
\phi
-stable representative of an element of
X_{*}(T)
. Hence
x^{\prime}\phi^{c}(I_{N,n})(x^{\prime})^{-1}\subseteq I_{N,n+1}
for any such
c
. Let
i\in I_{N,n}
and let
\phi_{x}:g\mapsto x\phi(g)x^{-1}
. Let
\delta=i\phi_{x}(i)\dotsm\phi_{x}^{c-1}(i)
. Then
\delta\in I_{N,n}
and
\delta^{-1}ix\phi(\delta)=x\phi(\phi_{x}^{c-1}(i))=\phi_{x}^{c}(i)x=x^{\prime}\phi^{c}(i)(x^{\prime})^{-1}x\in I_{N,n+1}x
which proves the assertion for
I^{\prime}=I_{N,n}
. The argument for
I^{\prime}=I_{\bar{N},n}
is analogous, using that
xI_{\bar{N}}x^{-1}\supseteq I_{\bar{N}}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.31
.

By Proposition
6.29
we only need to show injectivity. More precisely, it is enough to show that there is an
N_{0}
such that

\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht)\longrightarrow\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N_{0})

is injective on isomorphism classes of
k
-valued points. Let
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be
k
-valued points of the left hand side. We trivialize the
G
-torsors underlying
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, thus they are of the form
(LG_{k},b\phi)
and
(LG_{k},b^{\prime}\phi)
for some
b,b^{\prime}\in LG(k)
bounded by
\mu
. Choosing the trivializations compatibly with the isomorphism of truncated
G
-shtukas, we may assume that
b^{\prime}=h_{1}bh_{2}
with
h_{1},h_{2}\in L^{+}_{N}G(k)=K_{N}
for some
N
, yet to be determined. Let
b_{0}
be a representative of a fundamental element in
[b]
. Let
S
be as in Corollary
6.34
. Then there is a
g\in G(\breve{F})
with
g^{-1}b_{0}\phi(g)=b
, i.e.,
g\in X_{\leq\mu}(b_{0})
. By the corollary, we may choose
g\in\coprod_{\xi\in S}G(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{F}})\xi(t)G(\mathcal{O}_{\breve{F}})
. We have

gb^{\prime}\phi(g)^{-1}=(gh_{1}g^{-1})b_{0}\phi(gh_{2}g^{-1})^{-1}.

Define

(6.4.1)

C:=\max_{\alpha,\xi\in S}\langle\alpha,\xi\rangle,

where
\alpha
runs over all roots. For every
C<n\in\mathbb N
, every
g\in\coprod_{\xi\in S}L^{+}G(k)\xi(t)L^{+}G(k)
and every
h\in K_{n}
we have
ghg^{-1}\in K_{n-C}
. Thus if we choose
N
larger than
C
, we have
gb^{\prime}\phi(g)^{-1}\in gK_{N}bK_{N}g^{-1}\subseteq K_{N-C}b_{0}K_{N-C}
. In particular, from Lemma
6.35
for
n=1
we obtain that
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are isogenous as soon as
N>C
since then the right hand side is contained in
[b_{0}]
. Assume now that
N>2C
. By the lemma,
gb^{\prime}\phi(g)^{-1}\in I_{N-C-1}b_{0}I_{N-C-1}
can be written as
ib_{0}\phi(i)^{-1}
for some
i\in I_{N-C-1}\subseteq K_{N-C-1}
. Hence

b^{\prime}=(g^{-1}ig)b\phi(g^{-1}i^{-1}g)
and
g^{-1}ig
is in
K_{N-2C-1}\subseteq K
. Thus
b^{\prime}
is
K
-
\phi
-conjugate to
b
, and the local
G
-shtukas
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are isomorphic.
∎

Remark 6.36
.

The least possible
N_{1}
such that any two
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\in\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht)(k)
mapping to the same point in
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\Sht,N_{1})
are isogenous is called the isogeny cutoff for the datum
(G,\mu)
. Similarly, the least possible
N_{0}
as in Theorem
6.31
is called the isomorphism cutoff for
(G,\mu)
. From the proof of the theorem, we obtain explicit bounds
C+1
for the isogeny cutoff and
2C+1
for the isomorphism cutoff in terms of the set
S
in Corollary
6.34
.

7.
Truncated displays

In this section, we apply the general results to displays and their truncations. Many of the results of this section just rephrase results from Lau in
[
Lau21
]
and Daniels in
[
Dan21
]
in our language. Exceptions are the geometrization of the theory obtained by defining and using the various stacks
R^{\Disp}
,
R^{\Disp,N}
, or
R^{\Disp,N,n}
.

In this section, set
\kappa:=\mathbb{F}_{p}
the field with
p
-elements and
O=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Choose an algebraic closure
{\bar{\kappa}}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\textup{ur}}
be the ring of integers in the corresponding maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Let
R
denote a
p
-adically complete ring, let
W(R)
be the ring of
p
-adic Witt vectors. For every
N\geq 1
we denote by
W_{N}(R)
its truncation, so that
W_{1}(R)=R
. We also set
W_{\infty}(R):=W(R)
. For
1\leq N\leq\infty
we denote by
V\colon W_{N}(R)\to W_{N}(R)
the Verschiebung and set
I_{N}(R):=V(W_{N}(R))
which is an ideal of
W_{N}(R)
such that
W_{N}(R)/I_{N}(R)\cong R
.

We will often consider
p
-adically formal stacks. These are defined as stacks on the site of bounded
p
-adically complete rings endowed with the
p
-completely flat topology.

7.1.
The truncated display stacks

Let
1\leq N\leq\infty
. Every element of
I_{N}(R)
is of the form
V(x)
for a unique
x\in W_{N}(R)
. Define a filtration on
W_{N}(R)
(in the sense of Definition
2.7
) by
\Fil^{i}:=I_{N+1}(R)
for
i>0
, where we set
\infty+1:=\infty
, with maps

\ldots\mathrel{\smash{\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}}}\Fil^{i+1}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}}}\Fil^{i}\mathrel{\smash{\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}}}\ldots\mathrel{\smash{\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}}}\Fil^{1}\longrightarrow\Fil^{0}=W_{N}(R)

defined as follows. For
i\geq 1
the maps
\Fil^{i+1}\to\Fil^{i}
are given by multiplication with
p
. The map
\Fil^{1}\to W_{N}(R)
is the truncation map
I_{N+1}(R)\to I_{N}(R)
followed by the inclusion
I_{N}(R)\to W(R)
. So the image of
\Fil^{1}\to W_{N}(R)
is
I_{N}(R)
.
The multiplication
\Fil^{i}\times\Fil^{j}\to\Fil^{i+j}
for
i,j\geq 1
is given by

I_{N+1}(R)\times I_{N+1}(R)\longrightarrow I_{N+1}(R),\qquad V(x)\cdot V(y):=V(xy),\quad\text{for $x,y\in W(R)$.}

This defines a filtration since one has
V(x)V(y)=pV(xy)
for all
x,y\in W_{N}(R)
.

We get the attached
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
W_{N}(R)
-algebra
\Rees(W_{N}(R))
and the attached Rees stack

\Re(W_{N}(R))=[\mathbb{G}_{m,W(R)}\backslash\Spec\Rees(W_{N}(R))],

which we call the
N
-truncated Rees–Witt stack
. For
N=\infty
, it it simply called the
Rees–Witt stack
. For all
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
we obtain a closed immersion of algebraic stacks

\Re(W_{N}(R))\longrightarrow\Re(W_{N^{\prime}}(R)).

Next we endow the filtered ring
(W_{N}(R),(\Fil^{i})_{i})
with the structure of a frame (Definition
4.1
). For this, we start with the following remark.

Remark 7.1
.

The quotient algebra
\Rees(W_{N}(R))^{-}
, which defines the repeller locus, is defined in
\Rees(W_{N}(R))
by the ideal
I^{+}
with
I^{+}_{i}=p^{-i}I_{N}(R)=I_{N}(R)^{-i+1}
for
i\leq 0
by Remark
2.13
and because we have
I_{N}(R)^{i}=p^{i-1}I_{N}(R)
for all
i\geq 1
.

Let us now define a frame first for
N=\infty
. Let
\phi
be the Frobenius endomorphism on
W(R)
and define
\sigma\colon\Spec W(R)\to\Re(W(R))
inducing
\phi
by Lemma
4.2
as follows. Let
\sigma^{*}\colon\phi^{*}\Rees(W(R))\to\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}W(R)^{\otimes i}
with
\sigma^{*}_{-1}(1\otimes t)=p\in W(R)^{\otimes-1}
,
\sigma^{*}_{0}(1\otimes a)=\phi(a)
, and
\sigma^{*}_{i}(1\otimes V(a))=a
for
a\in W(R)
and
i\geq 1
. For
i\geq 1
, the image of
\Rees(A)_{i}\subseteq I^{+}
under
\sigma^{*}_{i}
generates
W(R)
and hence
\sigma
factors through a morphism
\sigma\colon\Spec W(R)\to\Re(W(r))^{\neq-}
by Lemma
4.2
using Remark
7.1
.

Hence we obtain the
Witt frame

(W(R),(\Fil^{i})_{i},\sigma)
, reproducing Lau’s definition in
[
Lau21
]
of the Witt frame in our language. We get the attached frame stack (Definition
4.6
)

(7.1.1)

R^{\Disp}:={\mathcal{F}}(W(R),(\Fil^{i})_{i},\sigma)

which we call the
displayfication of
R
.

Now let us consider the truncated case, i.e.,
N<\infty
. In this case, the Frobenius
\phi
induces a ring endomorphism
\phi\colon W_{N}(R)\to W_{N}(R)
only if
R
is of characteristic
p
. Hence let us assume this for now, see also Remark
7.2
below for a variant for
p
-adically complete rings.

Let
\sigma\colon\Spec W_{N}(R)\to\Re(W_{N}(R))
be defined as in the non-truncated case above. In other words, it is the unique map of stacks making the diagram

\textstyle{\Spec W_{N}(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\Re(W_{N}(R))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spec W(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\Re(W(R))}

commutative. We obtain a frame structure on
\Re(W_{N})
which we call the
N
-truncated Witt frame
. The attached frame stack is denoted by

(7.1.2)

R^{\Disp,N}:={\mathcal{F}}(W_{N}(R),(\Fil^{i})_{i},\sigma)

and is called the
N
-truncated displayfication of
R
. We also set
R^{\Disp,\infty}:=R^{\Disp}
.
Note that the truncated Witt frame is not a quotient frame of the Witt frame in the sense of Definition
4.5
. But still we have for all
1\leq N\leq N^{\prime}\leq\infty
a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Re(W_{N}(R))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{\Disp,N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Re(W_{N^{\prime}}(R))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{\Disp,N^{\prime}}.}

Remark 7.2
.

We can also define a variant of
R^{\Disp,N}
if
R
is
p
-adically complete following ideas from Hoff
[
Hof25
]
and
[
Hof23
]
. In this case, the Frobenius
\phi
induces maps
W_{N}(R)\to W_{n}(R)
for all
n<N
. We denote this induced map again by
\phi
. Thus we fix integers
1\leq n<N<\infty
. In this case we obtain as above a map
\sigma\colon W_{n}(R)\to\Re(W_{N}(R))
. We also denote by
\tau
the composition of
\Spec W_{n}(R)\to W_{N}(R)
with the canonical map
\Spec W_{N}(R)\to\Re(W_{N}(R))
(
2.2.4
) and then define a stack
R^{\Disp,N,n}
as the coequalizer of
\sigma
and
\tau
.

Let us endow

{\mathcal{N}}:=\{\,(N,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\ ;\ 1\leq n<N\,\}

with the product partial order

(N,n)\leq(N^{\prime},n^{\prime}){:}\Leftrightarrow N\leq N^{\prime}\text{\ and\ }n\leq n^{\prime}.

Then one obtains for all pairs
(N,n)\leq(N^{\prime},n^{\prime})
a map

R^{\Disp,N,n}\longrightarrow R^{\Disp,N^{\prime},n^{\prime}}

The following result describes
1
-truncated displays. It is a stacky reformulation of a result of Lau
[
Lau21
, 2.1.7]
.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
R
be a ring of characteristic
p
.

(1)

One has a pushout diagram of Adams stacks

\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\sqcup B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\sqcup\infty}
\scriptstyle{\id_{R}\sqcup\Frob_{R}}
\textstyle{[\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}_{R}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\Re(W_{1}(R)).}

(2)

There is an isomorphism of stacks, functorial in
R
,

(7.1.3)

R^{\Zip}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}R^{\Disp,1}.

Pulling back
\GL_{n}
-bundles via (
7.1.3
) gives an alternative proof of
[
Lau21
, 3.6.4]
.

Proof.

Recall the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
D_{R}
from (
6.3.6
). It is straight forward to see that one has an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded algebras
D_{R}\cong\Rees(W_{1}(R))
which implies
(1)
after forming quotients by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. This is compatible with pushouts, since forming quotients can be itself expressed as a colimit. The isomorphism
D_{R}\cong\Rees(W_{1}(R))
also shows
(2)
by (
6.3.7
) and the definition of
R^{\Disp,1}
.
∎

7.2.
Classification of truncated
G
-displays

From now on let
G
be a smooth affin group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Our next goal is to apply the general results to describe moduli spaces of
G
-bundles on the stacks defined above.

Since
R
is a
p
-adically complete ring,
W_{N}(R)
is
I_{N}(R)
-adically complete and
p
-adically complete
[
Zin02
, Prop. 3]
. In particular
(W_{N}(R),I_{N}(R))
is a henselian pair and hence
\Rees(W_{N}(R))
is henselian (Definition
3.1
). Therefore Corollary
3.3
gives the following result, which for
G=\GL_{n}
can be seen as a reformulation for the existence of normal decompositions for displays as in
[
Lau21
, 3.3]
.

Proposition 7.4
.

There is an isomorphism of pointed sets

H^{1}(\Re(W_{N}(R)),G)\overset{\sim}{\to}H^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R},G).

Let us now study the following prestacks of
G
-bundles.

Definition 7.5
.

For every
p
-adically complete ring
R
we set

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(W)(R)

\displaystyle:=\Bun_{G}(\Re(W(R))),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(W_{N})(R)

\displaystyle:=\Bun_{G}(\Re(W_{N}(R))),\qquad 1\leq N\leq\infty,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp)(R)

\displaystyle:=\Bun_{G}(R^{\Disp}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp,N,n)(R)

\displaystyle:=\Bun_{G}(R^{\Disp,N,n}),\qquad 1\leq n<N<\infty,

if
pR=0
, we also set

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp,N)(R)

\displaystyle:=\Bun_{G}(R^{\Disp,N}),\qquad 1\leq N\leq\infty.

Lemma 7.6
.

All these prestacks are stacks for the
p
-completely flat topology.

For
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp,N)
this just means that it is an fpqc-stack on the catagory of all
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras.

Proof.

The prestacks
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(W_{N})
are stacks: For
N=\infty
this follows from
[
Dan21
, 3.5]
since one has a Tannakian description of
G
-bundles by
[
Wed24
, A.30]
. The truncated case can be shown similarly, only that the used descent result by Lau
[
Lau21
, 4.3.2]
is easier to prove in the truncated case. Now
R\mapsto\Bun_{G}(W_{N}(R))
is also a stack for all
1\leq N<\infty
(use for instance
[
BH20
, 2.12]
). This shows that the prestacks
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp)
,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp,N,n)
,
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}(\Disp,N)
are all equalizers of stacks, hence they are also stacks.
∎

Now fix a conjugacy class
[\mu]
of cocharacters of
G
defined over some finite flat extension dvr
O_{[\mu]}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. If
G
is reductive, we let
O_{[\mu]}
its ring of definition, a finite étale extension of
O
by Remark
3.15
.

Definition 7.7
.

Let
R
be a
p
-adically complete ring. A
G
-display over
R
is defined as a
G
-bundle over
R^{\Disp}
. An
(N,n)
-truncated
G
-display over
R
is a
G
-bundle over
R^{\Disp,N,n}
. If
R
is of characteristic
p
, then an
N
-truncated
G
-display over
R
is a
G
-bundle over
R^{\Disp,N}
.

We say that a (truncated or not) display
{\mathscr{E}}
is of type
[\mu]
if it is étale locally isomorphic to
{\mathscr{E}}_{[\mu]}
(Definition
3.8
).

We obtain substacks
\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}_{G}^{[\mu]}(\bigstar)
for
\bigstar\in\{W,W_{N},\Disp,(\Disp,N,n),(\Disp,N)\}
over
O_{[\mu]}
. If
G
is reductive, these are open and closed substacks (
3.2.5
).

Remark 7.8
.

As already mentioned (without proof) by Hoff in
[
Hof25
, 2.8]
, the groupoid of pairs of type
(h,d)
over
R
in the sense of loc. cit. Definition 2.2 is equivalent to the groupoid of
\GL_{h}
-bundles over
\Re(W(R))
of type
[\mu_{d}]
, where
[\mu_{d}]
is the conjugacy class of the standard minuscule cocharacter

\mu_{d}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\GL_{h},\qquad t\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}tI_{d}&0\\
0&I_{h-d}\end{pmatrix}.

This implies easily that the groupoid of displays of type
(h,d)
over
R
is equivalent to the groupoid of
\GL_{h}
-bundles of type
[\mu_{d}]
over
R^{\Disp}
.
Christopher Lang gives a proof of these statements in Appendix
C
.

A similar result with the same proof holds for
N
-truncated pairs,
(N,n)
-truncated displays, and
N
-truncated displays.

Next we give descriptions of all these stacks as quotient stacks, similarly as in the shtuka case in Chapter
6
. First recall the notion of truncated Witt loop groups. For each
p
-adically complete
R
-algebra we set

\displaystyle L^{W}G(R)

\displaystyle:=G(W(R)[1/p]),

\displaystyle L^{W,+}G(R)

\displaystyle:=G(W(R)),

\displaystyle L^{W,(N)}G(R)

\displaystyle:=G(W_{N}(R)),\qquad 1\leq N\leq\infty.

Hence
L^{W,(\infty)}G=L^{W,+}(G)
. Then
L^{W,(N)}G
is an affine group scheme. It is of finite type for
N<\infty
.

As in Section
3.3
we also have a display group
E_{N}^{W}(G,[\mu])
attached to
R\mapsto\Re(W_{N}(R))
for all
1\leq N\leq\infty
which by Proposition
3.24
is a formal affine group scheme over
\Spf\mathbb{Z}_{p}
which is smooth if
N<\infty
. Moreover
E_{\infty}(G,[\mu])=\lim_{N<\infty}E_{N}(G,[\mu])
. The general results Theorem
3.29
and Theorem
4.12
then specialize to the following description of the various stacks of
G
-bundles.

Theorem 7.9
.

One has equivalences of stacks for the
p
-completely flat topology on the category of bounded
p
-adically complete rings

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(W_{N})

\displaystyle\cong BE_{N}^{W}(G,[\mu]),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Disp)

\displaystyle\cong[E_{\infty}^{W}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{W,+}G],

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Disp,{N,n})

\displaystyle\cong[E_{N}^{W}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{W,(n)}G].

Further, one has equivalences of stacks for the fpqc-topology on the category of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Disp,{N})

\displaystyle\cong[E_{N}^{W}(G,[\mu])\backslash L^{W,(N)}G].

Proof.

We have to check that the Assumptions
4.10
are satisfied. For
(a)
this is clear in the truncated case since
R\mapsto W_{N}(R)
,
R\mapsto I_{N}(R)
are represented as schemes by an affine space for
N<\infty
. For
N=\infty
they are represented by a cofiltered limit of affine spaces which is an affine scheme. We have already remarked above that Condition
(b)
is satisfied since
W_{N}(R)
is
I_{N}(R)
-adically complete. Finally, Condition
(c)
is satisfied by
[
BH20
, 2.12]
).
∎

Corollary 7.10
.

One has

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(W)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N<\infty}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(W_{N}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Disp)

\displaystyle=\lim_{(N,n)\in{\mathcal{N}}^{\opp}}\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Disp,{N,n}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{B}\textup{un}}^{[\mu]}_{G}(\Disp)
